      "content": "This page provides information about upcoming changes to Coinbase Prime Broker.\nCross margin overview: spot trading and Vault breakdown\nGet Prime Cross Margin Overview\nwill return\nspot_position_breakdown\non each entry in\nmargin_summary.risk_netting_info.xm_positions\n.\nEach item has\nposition_type\n(\nXM_POSITION_TYPE_SPOT_TRADING\nor\nXM_POSITION_TYPE_SPOT_VAULT\n),\nbalance\nand\nnotional_balance\n.\nThe items for an asset sum to that asset\u2019s\nspot_balance\nand\nspot_balance_notional\n.\nAssets with no spot balance return an empty list. Clients that switch on\nposition_type\nshould ignore values they don\u2019t recognize; more types may be added.\nAdvanced Transfer MATCHED state\nList Advanced Transfers\nwill report\nMATCHED\nwhile a transfer is matched and settlement has not started.\nThe happy path is\nCREATED\n,\nMATCHED\n,\nPROCESSING\n,\nDONE\n.\nCreate Advanced Transfer\nstill returns\nCREATED\n. Live state, including\nMATCHED\n, comes from List.\nA transfer in\nMATCHED\ncannot be canceled.\nClients that switch on\nstate\nshould handle\nMATCHED\n. Filtering List with\nCREATED\nomits matched transfers.\nAdvanced Transfer idempotency key\nCreate Advanced Transfer\nwill accept an optional\nidempotency_key\n.\nA retry with the same\nidempotency_key\nreturns the original transfer.\nThe response\nid\nis the transfer\u2019s transaction id. It is not the\nidempotency_key\n, and a client-supplied\nadvanced_transfer.id\nis not used.\nAdvanced Transfer Coinbase fees\nGet Transaction and List Advanced Transfer Transactions will return Coinbase fees on the\nTransaction\nobject.\nOn the transfer,\ncoinbase_fees\nis set (\namount\n,\nsymbol\n,\nwallet_id\n) and\nfees\nis\n0\n.\nOn the send, the same charge is on\nfees\nand\nfee_symbol\n, and\ncoinbase_fees\nis omitted. When you total fees, do not add\ncoinbase_fees\nfrom the transfer to the send\u2019s\nfees\n, or you will count the charge twice.\nA wallet transaction list includes the fees paid by that wallet. Get Transaction and the portfolio transaction list include every fee paid in the requesting portfolio. Fees paid in the counterparty\u2019s portfolio are omitted.\nCrypto and fiat sends that belong to the transfer use those transfer and send fields. A bank withdrawal that is not part of the transfer is unchanged.\nList Advanced Transfers\nhas no fee fields.\nSee\nGet Transaction\n,\nList transactions associated with an Advanced Transfer\n,\nList Portfolio Transactions\n, and\nList Wallet Transactions\n.\nSPREAD orders: trade a futures contract against spot or another contract\nThis change will be available in November 2026\nCreate Order\nand\nCreate Order Preview\nwill accept a new order type,\nSPREAD\n. A SPREAD order works two legs, a US futures contract against its spot underlying (for example\nBIP-20DEC30-CDE\nagainst\nBTC-USD\n) or against another contract of the same underlying, to capture a target spread between them. SPREAD orders are enabled per portfolio; contact your account representative to turn them on.\nRequest\nSet\ntype\nto\nSPREAD\nand provide\nspread_config\n:\nlimit\nand\nlimit_type\n(\nSPREAD_LIMIT_TYPE_BPS\nor\nSPREAD_LIMIT_TYPE_USD\n): the worst acceptable spread (futures price minus spot price), signed.\nmax_delta_size\nand\nmax_delta_type\n(\nMAX_DELTA_TYPE_SPOT\n,\nMAX_DELTA_TYPE_PERCENT\nor\nMAX_DELTA_TYPE_CONTRACT\n): the largest allowed difference between the two legs\u2019 filled sizes while the order works.\nprimary_leg\nand\nsecondary_leg\n, each with\nproduct_id\n,\nside\nand\nsupport_passive_order\n. The legs take opposite sides and at least one must allow passive fills.\nproduct_id\nmust equal\nprimary_leg.product_id\n, and\nbase_quantity\nis in the primary leg\u2019s units: contracts for a futures product, base units for spot.\ntime_in_force\nmust be\nGOOD_UNTIL_CANCELLED\nor\nGOOD_UNTIL_DATE_TIME\n.\nLeave\nside\nunset; each leg carries its own side.\nlimit_price\n,\nquote_value\n,\ndisplay_base_size\n,\ndisplay_quote_size\n,\nstop_price\n,\npost_only\n,\nis_raise_exact\n,\nis_buy_exact\nand the TWAP/VWAP/PEG fields are rejected on SPREAD orders.\nResponse\nOrder previews return\nspread_preview\nwith the submitted configuration, the spread product\u2019s\ngroup_product_id\n(for example\nBIP-20DEC30-CDE:BTC-USD\n) and, for each leg,\nleg_commission\n,\nquote_value\n,\nbest_bid\n,\nbest_ask\n,\naverage_filled_price\nand\nslippage\n.\ncommission\nis the sum of the two leg commissions.\nPreviews of futures and SPREAD orders also return\ncurrent_margin_utilization\n,\ncurrent_margin_utilization_ratio\n,\nprojected_margin_utilization\nand\nprojected_margin_utilization_ratio\n.\nGet Order\n,\nList Orders\nand\nList Open Orders\nreport SPREAD orders with\ntype\nSPREAD\n,\nproduct_type\nBASIS\nand\nspread_details\n: the submitted configuration, the\ngroup_product_id\n, the\nrealized_spread\nand each leg\u2019s fills (\nfilled_quantity\n,\naverage_filled_price\n,\ncommission\n). Clients that switch on\ntype\nor\nproduct_type\nshould handle these values.\nOPTION products visible by default in List Portfolio Products\nThis change will be available on October 26 2026\nList Portfolio Products\nwill return OPTION products by default for all portfolios. When no\nproduct_type\nfilter is specified, the response will include OPTION products alongside SPOT and FUTURE.\nWhat to expect\nOptions add many instruments, so a single page will no longer hold every product. Clients that read the full list should follow the\npagination\ncursor as described in\nPagination\nrather than relying on one request returning everything.\nThe\nproduct_type\nvalue\nOPTION\nwill appear in responses that previously contained only\nSPOT\nand\nFUTURE\n. Clients that switch on\nproduct_type\nshould handle this value.\nVisibility does not grant trading access. Placing orders on OPTION products still requires the corresponding derivatives entitlements on the portfolio; orders on non-entitled products are rejected at order entry.\nHow to filter for SPOT only products\nTo receive only spot products, pass an explicit filter and a page size large enough to cover them:\nGET /v1/portfolios/{portfolio_id}/products?product_type=SPOT&limit=1000\nlimit\naccepts values up to 3000. Use\nproduct_type=OPTION\nto retrieve only options.\nMore specific onchain transaction and activity types\nThis change will be available on January 15, 2027\nWe are adding more specific types for onchain wallet deposits and rewards. This change affects transaction and activity responses:\nOnchain wallet deposits that currently return the transaction type\nDEPOSIT\nwill return\nONCHAIN_DEPOSIT\n.\nOnchain rewards that currently return the transaction type\nREWARD\nwill return\nONCHAIN_REWARD\n.\nActivities for onchain wallet deposits that currently return\nACTIVITY_TYPE_DEPOSIT\nwill return\nACTIVITY_TYPE_ONCHAIN_DEPOSIT_TRANSACTION\n.\nReward activities that currently return\nOTHER_ACTIVITY_TYPE\nwill return\nACTIVITY_TYPE_REWARD\n.\nUpdate integrations that parse or filter these values. After this change, filtering transactions with\ntypes=DEPOSIT\nor\ntypes=REWARD\nwill no longer include onchain deposits or onchain rewards. To retrieve those transactions, use\ntypes=ONCHAIN_DEPOSIT\nor\ntypes=ONCHAIN_REWARD\n, respectively.\nPrime webhooks\nWe are releasing Prime webhooks for transactions and activities. Prime sends an HTTPS POST to your endpoint when a transaction or activity is created or updated.\nThe first event types are:\nEvent type\nWhen Prime sends it\nprime.transaction.created\nA transaction is created.\nprime.transaction.updated\nA transaction status changes.\nprime.activity.created\nAn activity is created.\nprime.activity.updated\nAn activity status or its approvals change.\nWe are also releasing a set of REST API endpoints for managing webhook integrations. You will be able to:\nCreate, retrieve, list, update, and delete subscriptions.\nDiscover the available event types.\nRotate a subscription\u2019s HMAC signing secret.\nSend simulated events to test an integration.\nReview delivery logs and individual delivery attempts.\nView delivery statistics for a subscription or portfolio.\nReplay a previously published event.\nWebhook deliveries support two signature verification methods:\nHMAC-SHA256:\nVerify the\nX-Coinbase-Signature\nheader with the per-subscription signing secret returned when you create the subscription. Store this secret securely because it is returned only once.\nEd25519:\nVerify the\nX-Coinbase-Signature-Ed25519\nheader with the Coinbase public key from the JWKS endpoint. Use\nX-Coinbase-Signature-Key-Id\nto select the correct key.\nVerify one of these signatures before processing a delivery, and return HTTP\n200\nto acknowledge the event.\nAdvanced Transfer trade_date Removed from the Public Schema\nThe optional\ntrade_date\nfield on Advanced Transfer\nblind_match_metadata\nis being removed from the public OpenAPI specification, generated SDKs, and API reference. New integrations should omit this field. Settlement date and time are unchanged. See\nCreate Advanced Transfer\n.\nREST API Error Responses\nWe are improving REST API error readability and expanding the\nPrime REST API\nOpenAPI specification to document error responses across endpoints. Error bodies use a consistent JSON structure with\ncode\n,\nmessage\n,\nsubcode\n, and\ntrace_id\nfields. See the\nError Dictionary\nfor a full list of\ncode\nand\nsubcode\nvalues. Each endpoint documents the HTTP status codes it can return (such as\n400\n,\n401\n,\n403\n,\n404\n, and\n500\n) along with typed error response schemas in the API reference.\nField\nDescription\ncode\nThe general error category. Maps to a fixed HTTP status code.\nsubcode\nA specific, actionable reason for the failure. Use this for programmatic error handling.\nmessage\nA human-readable description of what went wrong.\ntrace_id\nA unique identifier for this request. Include it when contacting support.\nNote:\nThe\ntrace_id\nvalue shown is an illustrative placeholder. Every real request returns a unique\ntrace_id\n.\nPreviously, errors returned only a\nmessage\nfield. Responses now include structured codes:\nBefore:\n\"message\"\n:\n\"Invalid argument: portfolio_id\"\nAfter:\n{\n\"code\"\n:\n\"VALIDATION_ERROR\"\n,\n\"subcode\"\n:\n\"VALIDATION_PORTFOLIO_ID_INVALID\"\n,\n\"message\"\n:\n\"Invalid argument: portfolio_id\"\n,\n\"trace_id\"\n:\n\"2644254813958296634\"\n}\nStatus-specific and endpoint-specific\nsubcode\nvalues help you identify the failure reason programmatically. Browse any endpoint in the\nPrime REST API reference\nto view its documented error responses in the playground and response schemas.\nWebSocket Products Channel Product Type\nWe are adding a\nproduct_type\nattribute to the products returned on the\nproducts channel\nof the Prime WebSocket feed, along with an optional\nproduct_type\nfilter on subscribe.\nEvery product in\nsnapshot\nand\nupdate\nmessages will include a\nproduct_type\nfield, for example\nSPOT\n,\nFUTURE\n, or\nOPTION\n. Products whose type is not reported by the upstream service will be returned as\nUNKNOWN_PRODUCT_TYPE\n.\nSubscribe requests will accept an optional\nproduct_type\nfield. When set, the snapshot and all subsequent updates contain only products of that type. When omitted, every product is returned, so existing subscriptions are unaffected. Values are case-sensitive, and an unrecognized value is rejected with an error.\nproduct_type\nis not included in the signature payload, so no change to request signing is required.\nRequest (new field):\n{\n\"type\"\n:\n\"subscribe\"\n,\n\"channel\"\n:\n\"products\"\n,\n\"access_key\"\n:\n\"ACCESS_KEY\"\n,\n\"api_key_id\"\n:\n\"SVC_ACCOUNTID\"\n,\n\"timestamp\"\n:\n\"TIMESTAMP\"\n,\n\"passphrase\"\n:\n\"PASSPHRASE\"\n,\n\"signature\"\n:\n\"SIGNATURE\"\n,\n\"portfolio_id\"\n:\n\"PORTFOLIO_ID\"\n,\n\"product_ids\"\n: [],\n\"product_type\"\n:\n\"SPOT\"\n}\nResponse (new field):\n{\n\"channel\"\n:\n\"products\"\n,\n\"events\"\n: [\n{\n\"type\"\n:\n\"snapshot\"\n,\n\"products\"\n: [\n{\n\"product_id\"\n:\n\"BTC-USD\"\n,\n\"product_type\"\n:\n\"SPOT\"\n}\n]\n}\n]\n}\nPreviewUnstake Response Enhancements\nWe are adding new fields to the\nPreviewUnstake\nendpoint response to provide more detailed information about unstaking operations, including wallet details, timestamps, and per-validator unstaking estimates.\nThe following fields will be added to the\nPreviewUnstakeResponse\n:\nwallet_id\n- the wallet identifier\nwallet_address\n- the wallet address\ncurrent_timestamp\n- the current timestamp\nvalidators[]\n- an array of validator details, each containing:\nvalidator_address\n- the validator address\nestimated_unstaking_amount\n- the estimated amount being unstaked\nunstake_time_estimate_in_hours\n- estimated time to unstake in hours\nestimated_unstake_date\n- estimated date of unstake completion\nResponse (new fields):\n{\n\"wallet_id\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"wallet_address\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"current_timestamp\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"validators\"\n: [\n{\n\"validator_address\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"estimated_unstaking_amount\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"unstake_time_estimate_in_hours\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"estimated_unstake_date\"\n:\n\"string\"\n}\n]\n}\nGet Staking Status Endpoint\nThis change will be available in January 2026\nWe are introducing a new endpoint to retrieve the status of ongoing staking operations for an Ethereum wallet.\nPrime Rest API:\nGet Staking Status\n:\nGET /v1/portfolios/{portfolio_id}/wallets/{wallet_id}/staking/status\n- retrieve status of all active staking requests\nThis endpoint will allow you to view the current status of staking operations in progress, including estimated completion times and amounts for each validator. This feature will only support ETH.\nRequest:\n{\n\"portfolio_id\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"wallet_id\"\n:\n\"string\"\n}\nResponse:\n{\n\"portfolio_id\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"wallet_id\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"wallet_address\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"current_timestamp\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"validators\"\n: [\n{\n\"validator_address\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"statuses\"\n: [\n{\n\"amount\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"stake_type\"\n:\n\"STAKE_TYPE_INITIAL_DEPOSIT | STAKE_TYPE_TOP_UP\"\n,\n\"estimated_stake_date\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"estimated_hours_to_stake\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"requested_at\"\n:\n\"string\"\n}\n]\n}\n]\n}\nGet Unstaking Status Endpoint\nThis change will be available in late October to early November 2025\nWe are introducing a new endpoint to retrieve the status of ongoing unstaking operations for an Ethereum wallet.\nPrime Rest API:\nGet Unstaking Status\n:\nGET /v1/portfolios/{portfolio_id}/wallets/{wallet_id}/staking/unstake/status\n- retrieve status of all active unstaking requests\nThis endpoint will allow you to view the current status of unstaking operations in progress, including estimated completion times and amounts for each validator. This feature will initially only support ETH.\nRequest:\n{\n\"portfolio_id\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"wallet_id\"\n:\n\"string\"\n}\nResponse:\n{\n\"portfolio_id\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"wallet_id\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"wallet_address\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"current_timestamp\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"validators\"\n: [\n{\n\"validator_address\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"statuses\"\n: [\n{\n\"amount\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"unstake_type\"\n:\n\"UNSTAKE_TYPE_PARTIAL | UNSTAKE_TYPE_FULL\"\n,\n\"finishing_at\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"remaining_hours\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"requested_at\"\n:\n\"string\"\n,\n\"estimate_type\"\n:\n\"LIVE | INTERIM\"\n,\n\"estimate_description\"\n:\n\"string\"\n}\n]\n}\n]\n}\nPrime Multinetwork Support\nWe\u2019re expanding network support to make capital movement faster, cheaper, and easier:\nETH on Base and USDC on Base, Solana, Arbitrum, Optimism, and Avalanche C-Chain can now be transferred efficiently and at no cost within your trading balance on the Prime UI.\nAPI support will be available starting\nNovember 3rd\n.\nUsers will now be able to view and manage assets across multiple networks.\nWe will be updating the use of\nsymbol\nto represent assets on different blockchains. This will be available to users as\n\u201cnetwork_scoped_symbol\u201d\nin the List Entity Assets endpoint for each individual network supported for an asset.\nExample:\nIf a user wants to represent USDC on the Base network, they will use the\n\"network_scoped_symbol\"\nBASEUSDC.\nWhen a user calls the ListEntityAssets endpoint for USDC, the response will include a unique\n\u201cnetwork_scoped_symbol\n\u201d for USDC on each supported network, such as BASEUSDC for Base, OPTUSDC for Optimism, and so on.\nUsers can use the\n\u201cnetwork_scoped_symbol\u201d\nto get asset details specific to each network. Users can also add a new query parameter to see combined information for an asset across all supported networks denoted as:\n\u201cget_network_unified_transactions\u201d\n\u201dget_network_unified_wallets\u201d\n\u201cget_network_unified_activites\u201d\n\u201cunified_total_balances\u201d\nAll Endpoint Changes:\nList Portfolio Transactions\nUsers can enter a\n\u201cnetwork_scoped_symbol\u201d\nin the \u2018symbols\u2019 query parameter to get transactions for an asset on a specific network\nUsers can enter optional\n\u201cget_network_unified_transactions\u201d\nas a boolean query parameter to get transactions across all networks\nList Entity Activities\nUsers can enter\n\u201cnetwork_scoped_symbol\u201d\nin the \u2018symbols\u2019 query parameter to view activities for an asset on a specific network\nUsers can enter\n\u201cget_network_unified_activities\u201d\nas a boolean query parameter to get activities across all networks\nList Portfolio Activities\nUsers can enter\n\u201cnetwork_scoped_symbol\u201d\nin the \u2018symbols\u2019 query parameter to view portfolio activities for an asset on a specific network\nUsers can enter\n\u201cget_network_unified_activities\u201d\nas a boolean query parameter to get activities across all networks\nNetwork Scoped Symbols:\nAsset - Symbol\nNetwork Name\nNetwork_Scoped_Symbol\nUSDC\nEthereum\nusdc\nUSDC\nAvalanche C-Chain\navausdc\nUSDC\nOptimism\noptusdc\nUSDC\nBase\nbaseusdc\nUSDC\nSolana\nsplusdc\nUSDC\nArbitrum\narbusdc\nETH\nEthereum\neth\nETH\nBase\nbaseeth\nFinancing API Enhancements\nThis change will be published in late October to early November 2025\nWe are implementing changes to an existing endpoint and adding two new endpoints to enhance the API experience for users of the Trade Finance and Bilateral Lending products.\nList TF Obligations Endpoint\nThis new endpoint returns all open trade finance obligations.\n{\n\"obligations\": [\n{\n\"portfolio_id\": \"D7927E4F-DE95-4CF2-BD4C-00477BC25D70\",\n\"symbol\":\"USD\",\n\"amount\":\"20339.64\",\n\"notional_amount\": \"23928.87\",\n\"due_date\": \"2025-08-13T22:00:00Z\"\n}\n]\n}\nList Financing Eligible Assets Endpoint\nThis new endpoint returns all financing eligible assets and their associated trade finance credit utilization parameters.\n{\n\"assets\": [\n{\n\"asset\": \"XYO\",\n\"asset_adjustment\": \"0.4\",\n\"liability_adjustment\": \"3\"\n},\n{\n\"asset\": \"XRP\",\n\"asset_adjustment\": \"0.7\",\n\"liability_adjustment\": \"1.5\"\n},\n...\n]\n}\nList Interest Accruals Endpoint\nThis endpoint will now additionally include interest accruals for Bilateral Lending (in addition to Trade Finance and Portfolio Margin, as currently implemented)\nUser generated IDs maximum length requirement\nUpdate: This will be live mid-October 2025\nA maximum length requirement of 128 characters will be enforced for client generated IDs. Invalid argument errors will be raised if the IDs are longer than this limit.\nThe affected fields currently are:\nCreate Portfolio Allocations:\nallocation_id\n,\nallocation_leg_id\nCreate Portfolio Net Allocations:\nallocation_leg_id\n,\nnetting_id\nAccept Quote:\nclient_order_id\nCreate Order:\nclient_order_id\nCreate Quote Request:\nclient_quote_id\nFIX Market Data\nUpdate: This will be live in Q3\nWe will be adding level 2 market data on FIX 5.0 for Prime Trading Spot instruments. This includes OHLCV (Open, High, Low, Close, Volume), bids and offers, and trade history data.       "content": "Asset IDs are the integer representation for sending orders and cancels via actions. See\nhere\nfor more details.\nPerps\nPerpetual endpoints expect an integer for\nasset\n, which is the index of the coin found in the\nmeta\ninfo response. E.g.\nBTC = 0\non mainnet.\nBuilder-deployed perps expect\n100000 + perp_dex_index * 10000 + index_in_meta\n. For example,\ntest:ABC\non testnet has\nperp_dex_index = 1\n,\nindex_in_meta = 0\n,\nasset = 110000\n. Note that builder-deployed perps always have name in the format\n{dex}:{coin}\n.\nSpot\nSpot endpoints expect\n10000 + spotInfo[\"index\"]\nwhere\nspotInfo\nis the corresponding object in\nspotMeta\nthat has the desired quote and base tokens. For example, when submitting an order for\nPURR/USDC\n, the asset that should be used is\n10000\nbecause its asset index in the spot info is\n0\n.\nNote that spot ID is different from token ID, and that mainnet and testnet have different asset IDs. For example, for HYPE:\nMainnet token ID: 150\nMainnet spot ID: 107\nTestnet token ID: 1105\nTestnet spot ID: 1035\nOutcomes\nOutcomes share most implementation details with spot trading, with a different token representing each outcome side. However, the API representation of outcomes is different from both spot and perps.\nOutcome assets are derived from an\noutcome\nid plus a binary\nside\n. These are found in the\noutcomeMeta\ninfo response.\nFor an outcome with id\noutcome\nand side\nside\n:\nOnly side\n0\nand side\n1\nare valid.\nExample:\noutcome\n1\n, side\n0\n-> encoding\n10\nThe same\nencoding\nis used in three different representations:\nOutcome spot coin:\n#<encoding>\nOutcome token name:\n+<encoding>\nOutcome asset ID:\n100_000_000 + encoding\nExample:\n#10\n= outcome\n1\n, side\n0\n+10\n= the corresponding token name\n100000010\n= asset ID\nPrevious\nNotation\nNext\nTick and lot size\nLast updated\n8 months ago"

      "title": "Error responses",

      "last_checked": "2026-11-19T08:53:33.609033",
      "content": "Order and cancel errors are usually returned as a vector with same length as the batched request.\nBelow is a list of possible batched error responses:\nError source\nError type\nError string\nOrder\nTick\nPrice must be divisible by tick size.\nOrder\nMinTradeNtl\nOrder must have minimum value of $10.\nOrder\nMinTradeSpotNtl\nOrder must have minimum value of 10 {quote_token}.\nOrder\nPerpMargin\nInsufficient margin to place order.\nOrder\nReduceOnly\nReduce only order would increase position.\nOrder\nBadAloPx\nPost only order would have immediately matched, bbo was {bbo}.\nOrder\nIocCancel\nOrder could not immediately match against any resting orders.\nOrder\nBadTriggerPx\nInvalid TP/SL price.\nOrder\nMarketOrderNoLiquidity\nNo liquidity available for market order.\nOrder\nPositionIncreaseAtOpenInterestCap\nOrder would increase open interest while open interest is capped\nOrder\nPositionFlipAtOpenInterestCap\nOrder would increase open interest while open interest is capped\nOrder\nTooAggressiveAtOpenInterestCap\nOrder rejected due to price more aggressive than oracle while at open interest cap\nOrder\nOpenInterestIncrease\nOrder would increase open interest too quickly\nOrder\nInsufficientSpotBalance\n(Spot-only) Order has insufficient spot balance to trade\nOrder\nOracle\nOrder price too far from oracle\nOrder\nPerpMaxPosition\nOrder would cause position to exceed margin tier limit at current leverage\nCancel\nMissingOrder\nOrder was never placed, already canceled, or filled.\nImportant: Some errors are a deterministic function of the payload itself, and these are instead returned earlier as part of pre-validation. In this case only one error is returned for the entire payload, as some of these errors do not apply to a specific order or cancel.\nExamples include: empty batch of orders, non-reduce-only TP/SL orders, order too far from reference price, and some forms of tick size validation.\nFor API users that use batching, it's recommended to handle the case where a single error is returned for a batch of multiple orders. In this case, the response could be duplicated\nn\ntimes before being sent to the callback function, as the whole batch was rejected for this same reason.\nFor API users that use historical orders, a list of all the cancel / reject historical order statuses can be found\nhere\n.\nPrevious\nTimeouts and heartbeats\nNext\nSigning\nLast updated\n7 months ago"

    "https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/for-developers/api/exchange-endpoint": {
      "title": "Exchange endpoint",
      "hash": "86ceb2be448fd8028525d9f1312482c7cf36be3319593d68ed8df3fe08e23d8e",
      "last_checked": "2026-11-19T08:53:35.255787",
      "content": "Asset\nMany of the requests take asset as an input. For perpetuals this is the index in the\nuniverse\nfield returned by the\nmeta\nresponse. For spot assets, use\n10000 + index\nwhere\nindex\nis the corresponding index in\nspotMeta.universe\n. For example, when submitting an order for\nPURR/USDC\n, the asset that should be used is\n10000\nbecause its asset index in the spot metadata is\n0\n.\nSubaccounts and vaults\nSubaccounts and vaults do not have private keys. To perform actions on behalf of a subaccount or vault signing should be done by the master account and the vaultAddress field should be set to the address of the subaccount or vault. The basic_vault.py example in the Python SDK demonstrates this.\nExpires After\nSome actions support an optional field\nexpiresAfter\nwhich is a timestamp in milliseconds after which the action will be rejected. User-signed actions such as Core USDC transfer do not support the\nexpiresAfter\nfield. Note that actions consume 5x the usual address-based rate limit when canceled due to a stale\nexpiresAfter\nfield.\nSee the Python SDK for details on how to incorporate this field when signing.\nPlace an order\nPOST\nhttps://api.hyperliquid.xyz/exchange\nSee Python SDK for full featured examples on the fields of the order request.\nFor limit orders, TIF (time-in-force) sets the behavior of the order upon first hitting the book.\nALO (add liquidity only, i.e. \"post only\") will be canceled instead of immediately matching.\nIOC (immediate or cancel) will have the unfilled part canceled instead of resting.\nGTC (good til canceled) orders have no special behavior.\nClient Order ID (cloid) is an optional 128 bit hex string, e.g.\n0x1234567890abcdef1234567890abcdef\nHeaders\nName\nType\nDescription\nContent-Type\n*\nString\n\"application/json\"\nRequest Body\nName\nType\nDescription\naction\n*\nObject\n{\n\"type\": \"order\",\n\"orders\": [{\n\"a\": Number,\n\"b\": Boolean,\n\"p\": String,\n\"s\": String,\n\"r\": Boolean,\n\"t\": {\n\"limit\": {\n\"tif\": \"Alo\" | \"Ioc\" | \"Gtc\"\n} or\n\"trigger\": {\n\"isMarket\": Boolean,\n\"triggerPx\": String,\n\"tpsl\": \"tp\" | \"sl\"\n}\n},\n\"c\": Cloid (optional)\n}],\n\"grouping\": \"na\" | \"normalTpsl\" | \"positionTpsl\",\n\"builder\": Optional({\"b\": \"address\", \"f\": Number, \"s\": Optional([{\"b\": \"address\", \"s\": Number}])})\n}\nMeaning of keys:\na is asset\nb is isBuy\np is price\ns is size\nr is reduceOnly\nt is type\nc is cloid (client order id)\nMeaning of keys in optional builder argument:\nb is the address the should receive the additional fee\nf is the size of the fee in tenths of a basis point e.g. if f is 10, 1bp of the order notional will be charged to the user and sent to the builder\ns is an optional list of up to 5 sub-builders that receive part of the builder fee. In each entry b is the address, which must be an existing user, and s is the share of the builder fee in units of 0.001% e.g. if s is 50000, half of the builder fee is sent to that address. Shares must be positive and sum to at most 100%. The builder receives the remainder\nnonce\n*\nNumber\nRecommended to use the current timestamp in milliseconds\nsignature\n*\nObject\nvaultAddress\nString\nIf trading on behalf of a vault or subaccount, its Onchain address in 42-character hexadecimal format; e.g. 0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000\nexpiresAfter\nNumber\nTimestamp in milliseconds\n200: OK Successful Response (resting)\n200: OK Error Response\n200: OK Successful Response (filled)\nCancel order(s)\nPOST\nhttps://api.hyperliquid.xyz/exchange\nHeaders\nName\nType\nDescription\nContent-Type\n*\nString\n\"application/json\"\nRequest Body\nName\nType\nDescription\naction\n*\nObject\n{\n\"type\": \"cancel\",\n\"cancels\": [\n{\n\"a\": Number,\n\"o\": Number\n}\n],\n\"f\": Boolean // fast\n}\nMeaning of keys:\na is asset\no is oid (order id)\nnonce\n*\nNumber\nRecommended to use the current timestamp in milliseconds\nsignature\n*\nObject\nvaultAddress\nString\nIf trading on behalf of a vault or subaccount, its address in 42-character hexadecimal format; e.g. 0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000\nexpiresAfter\nNumber\nTimestamp in milliseconds\n200: OK Successful Response\n200: OK Error Response\nBoth\ncancel\nand\ncancelByCloid\nactions include an optional\nfast\nflag, encoded as\nf\nin the action. Orders with\nf: true\nare rejected if they refer to trigger orders. Currently\nfast\nhas no other effect. In a future network upgrade, cancel actions will be prioritized in the mempool if and only if\nfast = true\n.\nNote that\nf\nmust be skipped if false, i.e. actions hashed with\nf: false\nwill be rejected.\nCancel order(s) by cloid\nPOST\nhttps://api.hyperliquid.xyz/exchange\nHeaders\nName\nType\nDescription\nContent-Type\n*\nString\n\"application/json\"\nRequest Body\nName\nType\nDescription\naction\n*\nObject\n{\n\"type\": \"cancelByCloid\",\n\"cancels\": [\n{\n\"asset\": Number,\n\"cloid\": String\n}\n],\n\"f\": Boolean // fast\n}\nnonce\n*\nNumber\nRecommended to use the current timestamp in milliseconds\nsignature\n*\nObject\nvaultAddress\nString\nIf trading on behalf of a vault or subaccount, its address in 42-character hexadecimal format; e.g. 0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000\nexpiresAfter\nNumber\nTimestamp in milliseconds\n200: OK Successful Response\n200: OK Error Response\nSchedule cancel (dead man's switch)\nPOST\nhttps://api.hyperliquid.xyz/exchange\nHeaders\nName\nType\nDescription\nContent-Type\n*\nString\n\"application/json\"\nRequest Body\nName\nType\nDescription\naction\n*\nObject\n{\n\"type\": \"scheduleCancel\",\n\"time\": number (optional)\n}\nnonce\n*\nNumber\nRecommended to use the current timestamp in milliseconds\nsignature\n*\nObject\nvaultAddress\nString\nIf trading on behalf of a vault or subaccount, its address in 42-character hexadecimal format; e.g. 0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000\nexpiresAfter\nNumber\nTimestamp in milliseconds\nSchedule a cancel-all operation at a future time. Not including time will remove the scheduled cancel operation. The time must be at least 5 seconds after the current time. Once the time comes, all open orders will be canceled and a trigger count will be incremented. The max number of triggers per day is 10.       "content": "The API for deploying and operating builder-deployed perpetual dexs involves the following L1 actions:\nCopy\n// IMPORTANT: All lists of tuples should be lexographically sorted before signing\ntype\nPerpDeployAction\n=\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\nregisterAsset2\n:\nRegisterAsset2\n;\n}\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\nregisterAsset\n:\nRegisterAsset\n;\n}\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\nsetOracle\n:\nSetOracle\n;\n}\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\nsetFundingMultipliers\n:\nSetFundingMultipliers\n;\n}\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\nsetFundingInterestRates\n:\nSetFundingInterestRates\n;\n}\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\nsetFundingClamps\n:\nSetFundingClamps\n;\n}\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\nhaltTrading\n:\n{\ncoin\n:\nstring\n;\nisHalted\n:\nboolean\n};\n}\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\ninsertMarginTable\n:\nInsertMarginTable\n;\n}\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\nsetMarginTableIds\n:\nSetMarginTableIds\n;\n}\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\nsetFeeRecipient\n:\n{\ndex\n:\nstring\n;\nfeeRecipient\n:\naddress\n};\n}\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\nsetOpenInterestCaps\n:\nSetOpenInterestCaps\n;\n}\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\nsetSubDeployers\n:\n{\ndex\n:\nstring\n;\nsubDeployers\n:\nArray\n<\nSubDeployerInput\n>\n};\n}\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\nsetMarginModes\n:\nSetMarginModes\n;\n}\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\nsetDeployerFees\n:\nSetDeployerFees\n;\n}\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\nsetPerpAnnotation\n:\nSetPerpAnnotation\n;\n}\n|\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nperpDeploy\n\"\n;\ndisableDex\n:\nstring\n;\n};\n// User-signed action to transfer collateral between the user's DEX account and its backstop liquidator.\n// ntl must be positive and a multiple of 1_000_000_000.\ntype\nHip3LiquidatorTransferAction\n=\n{\ntype\n:\n\"\nhip3LiquidatorTransfer\n\"\n;\ndex\n:\nstring\n;\nntl\n:\nnumber\n;\n// Integer amount in millionths of a collateral token: 1_000_000_000 = 1,000 tokens\nisDeposit\n:\nboolean\n;\n// true deposits; false withdraws\n};\n/**\n* RegisterAsset2 can be called to initialize a new dex and register an asset at the same time.\n* If schema is not provided, then RegisterAsset can be called multiple times to register additional assets\n* for the provided dex.\n*\n@\nparam\nmaxGas\n- Max gas in native token wei. If not provided, then uses current deploy auction price.\n* If the max gas is 0, then a reserve deployment will be used.\n* A reserve deployment allows deployment at the current price of the gas auction, even if it has ended.\n* Currently, 7 reserve deployments are allowed.\n* IMPORTANT: A reserve deployment will be used regardless of whether the auction has completed, so deployers should query the auction status first.\n*\n@\nparam\nassetRequest\n- Contains new asset listing parameters. See RegisterAssetRequest2 below for details.\n*\n@\nparam\ndex\n- Name of the perp dex (2-4 lowercase characters)\n*\n@\nparam\nschema\n- Contains new perp dex parameters. See PerpDexSchemaInput below for details.\n*/\ntype\nRegisterAsset2\n=\n{\nmaxGas\n?:\nnumber\n;\nassetRequest\n:\nRegisterAssetRequest2\n;\ndex\n:\nstring\n;\nschema\n?:\nPerpDexSchemaInput\n;\n};\n// Same as RegisterAsset2 but uses RegisterAssetRequest\ntype\nRegisterAsset\n=\n{\nmaxGas\n?:\nnumber\n;\nassetRequest\n:\nRegisterAssetRequest\n;\ndex\n:\nstring\n;\nschema\n?:\nPerpDexSchemaInput\n;\n};\ntype\nRegisterAssetRequest2\n=\n{\ncoin\n:\nstring\n;\nszDecimals\n:\nnumber\n;\noraclePx\n:\nstring\n;\nmarginTableId\n:\nnumber\n;\nmarginMode\n:\n\"\nstrictIsolated\n\"\n|\n\"\nnoCross\n\"\n|\n\"\nnormal\n\"\n;\n// strictIsolated does not allow withdrawing of isolated margin from open positions\n};\ntype\nRegisterAssetRequest\n=\n{\ncoin\n:\nstring\n;\nszDecimals\n:\nnumber\n;\noraclePx\n:\nstring\n;\nmarginTableId\n:\nnumber\n;\nonlyIsolated\n:\nboolean\n;\n};\n/**\n* The markPxs outer list can be length 0, 1, or 2. The median of these inputs\n* along with the local mark price (median(best bid, best ask, last trade price))\n* is used as the new mark price update.\n*\n* SetOracle can be called multiple times but there must be at least 2.5 seconds between calls.\n*\n* Stale mark prices will be updated to the local mark price after 10 seconds of no updates.\n* This fallback counts as an update for purposes of the maximal update frequency.\n* This fallback behavior should not be relied upon. Deployers are expected to call setOracle every 3 seconds even with no changes.\n*\n* All prices are clamped to 10x the start of day value.\n* markPx moves are clamped to 1% from previous markPx.\n* markPx cannot be updated such that open interest would be 10x the open interest cap.\n*\n@\nparam\ndex\n- Name of the perp dex\n*\n@\nparam\noraclePxs\n- A list (sorted by key) of asset and oracle prices.\n*\n@\nparam\nmarkPxs\n- An outer list of inner lists (inner list sorted by key) of asset and mark prices.\n*\n@\nparam\nexternalPerpPxs\n- A list (sorted by key) of asset and external prices which prevent sudden mark price deviations.\n* Ideally externally determined by deployer, but could fall back to an EMA of recent mark prices.\n* Must include all assets.\n*/\ntype\nSetOracle\n=\n{\ndex\n:\nstring\n;\noraclePxs\n:\nArray\n<\n[\nstring\n,\nstring\n]\n>;\nmarkPxs\n:\nArray\n<\nArray\n<\n[\nstring\n,\nstring\n]\n>>;\nexternalPerpPxs\n:\nArray\n<\n[\nstring\n,\nstring\n]\n>;\n};\n/**\n*\n@\nparam\nfullName\n- Full name of the perp dex\n*\n@\nparam\ncollateralToken\n- Collateral token index\n*\n@\nparam\noracleUpdater\n- User to update oracles. If not provided, then deployer is assumed to be oracle updater.\n*/\ntype\nPerpDexSchemaInput\n=\n{\nfullName\n:\nstring\n;\ncollateralToken\n:\nint\n;\noracleUpdater\n?:\nstring\n;\n};\n/**\n* A sorted list of asset and funding multiplier.\n* Multipliers must be between 0 and 10 and are used to scale the funding rate.\n*/\ntype\nSetFundingMultipliers\n=\nArray\n<\n[\nstring\n,\nstring\n]\n>;\n/**\n* A sorted list of asset and 8 hour interest rate.\n* Interest rates must be between -0.01 and 0.01 and are used as the interest rate\n* component in the funding calculation.\n*/\ntype\nSetFundingInterestRates\n=\nArray\n<\n[\nstring\n,\nstring\n]\n>;\n/**\n* A sorted list of asset and 8 hour funding clamp.\n* Clamps must be between 0 and 0.01 and bound how far the funding rate can move away from the\n* average premium toward the interest rate. Defaults to 0.0003.\n*/\ntype\nSetFundingClamps\n=\nArray\n<\n[\nstring\n,\nstring\n]\n>;\n/**\n* A sorted of asset and margin table ids.\n* Margin table ids must be non-zero.\n*/\ntype\nSetMarginTableIds\n=\nArray\n<\n[\nstring\n,\nnumber\n]\n>;\n/**\n* Inserts margin table to dex\n*/\ntype\nInsertMarginTable\n=\n{\ndex\n:\nstring\n;\nmarginTable\n:\nRawMarginTable\n;\n};\n/**\n* marginTiers must be sorted in order of increasing lower bound and decreasing maxLeverage\n* marginTiers has a maximum length of 3.\n*/\ntype\nRawMarginTable\n=\n{\ndescription\n:\nstring\n;\nmarginTiers\n:\nArray\n<\nRawMarginTier\n>;\n};\n/**\n* lowerBound is a position notional value above which the leverage is constrained by maxLeverage\n*/\ntype\nRawMarginTier\n=\n{\nlowerBound\n:\nint\n;\nmaxLeverage\n:\nMaxLeverage\n;\n};\n/**\n* Max leverage is in the range [1, 50]\n*/\ntype\nMaxLeverage\n=\nnumber\n;\n/**\n* A sorted list of asset and open interest cap notionals.\n* Open interest caps must be at least the maximum of 1_000_000 (1 size unit of collateral asset) or half of the current open interest.\n* null removes the custom cap for an asset.\n*/\ntype\nSetOpenInterestCaps\n=\nArray\n<\n[\nstring\n,\nnumber\n|\nnull\n]\n>;\n/**\n* A modification to sub-deployer permissions\n*/\ntype\nSubDeployerInput\n=\n{\nvariant\n:\nstring\n;\n// corresponds to a variant of PerpDeployAction. For example, \"haltTrading\" or \"setOracle\"\nuser\n:\nString\n;\nallowed\n:\nboolean\n;\n// add or remove the subDeployer from the authorized set for the action variant\n};\n// A sorted list of (coin, marginMode). See RegisterAssetRequest2 for margin mode definitions.\ntype\nSetMarginModes\n=\nArray\n<\n[\nstring\n,\n\"\nstrictIsolated\n\"\n|\n\"\nnoCross\n\"\n|\n\"\nnormal\n\"\n]\n>;\n// Let the user normal rate be `x`. Let the user rate be `y` after accounting for aligned quote collateral.\n// In other words, `x = y` for non-aligned collateral.\n// User pays or receives rebate `P + D` where `P` goes to protocol and `D` goes to deployer.\n// If `x > 0` and `scale < 1`, `P = y` and `D = scale * x`\n// If `x > 0` and `scale > 1`, `P = y * scale` and `D = x * scale`\n// If `x < 0` and `scale < 1`, `P = y / (1 + scale)` and `D = y * scale / (1 + scale)`\n// If `x < 0` and `scale > 1`, `P = y / 2` and `D = y / 2`\n// On Mainnet, rate limited to one change per 30 days.\n// Note that there are currently no aligned quote assets on mainnet.\ntype\nSetDeployerFees\n=\nArray\n<\n[\nstring\n,\n{\nscale\n:\nstring\n;\n// Decimal string in [0.0, 3.0], or [0.0, 10.0) when growthMode is true\ngrowthMode\n:\nboolean\n;\n},\n]\n>;\n// category <= 15 characters\n// description <= 400 characters\n// displayName <= 9 characters\n// <= 2 keywords, each keyword <= 10 characters\ntype\nSetPerpAnnotation\n=\n{\ncoin\n:\nstring\n;\ncategory\n:\nstring\n;\ndescription\n:\nstring\n;\ndisplayName\n:\nstring\n|\nnull\n;\nkeywords\n:\nArray\n<\nstring\n>;\n};\nFor the following examples,\nfeeScale\nis the\nscale\nfield in\nSetDeployerFees\n. Assume a positive normal user fee of 1 unit and non-aligned collateral (\nx = y = 1\n).\nuserFeeToProtocol\nand\nuserFeeToDeployer\nare the resulting fee units charged to the user.\ngrowthMode\nfeeScale\nuserFeeToProtocol\nuserFeeToDeployer\nfalse\n\"0\"\n1\n0\nfalse\n\"0.5\"\n1\n0.5\nfalse\n\"1\"\n1\n1\nfalse\n\"3\"\n3\n3\ntrue\n\"0\"\n0.1\n0\ntrue\n\"0.5\"\n0.1\n0.05\ntrue\n\"1\"\n0.1\n0.1\ntrue\n\"3.01\"\n0.301\n0.301\ntrue\n\"9.99\"\n0.999\n0.999\nSee\nhttps://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/for-developers/api/info-endpoint/perpetuals#retrieve-information-about-the-perp-deploy-auction\nfor how to query for the perp deploy auction status.\nOpen interest caps\nBuilder-deployed perp markets are subject to two types of open interest caps: notional (sum of absolute position size times mark price) and size (sum of absolute position sizes).\nNotional open interest caps are enforced on the total open interest summed over all assets within the DEX, as well as per-asset. Perp deployers can set a custom open interest cap per asset, which is documented in\nhttps://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/for-developers/api/hip-3-deployer-actions\n.\nSize-denominated open interest caps are only enforced per-asset. Size-denominated open interest caps are currently a constant 1B per asset, so a reasonable default would be to set\nszDecimals\nsuch that the minimal size increment is $1-10 at the initial mark price.\nBackstop liquidator\nThe backstop liquidator address is\n0x4000000000000000000000000000000000000000 + {dex_index}\n. For example, the first HIP-3 DEX has backstop liquidator address\n0x4000000000000000000000000000000000000001\n.\nNode user clearinghouse states\nThe node flag\n--write-user-clearinghouse-states <dex>\nwrites clearinghouse states for the named HIP-3 DEX to hourly files at\n~/hl/data/dex_user_clearinghouse_states/hourly/<date>/<hour>\n, where\ndate\nis\nYYYYMMDD\n. Each line is a JSON array\n[time, messages]\n, where\ntime\nis the block time and\nmessages\nis either a single snapshot of every user on the DEX or a list of per-user diffs:\nEach\n<clearinghouseState>\nhas the same format as the\nclearinghouseState\ninfo request response for that DEX, denominated in the venue's collateral token. It only accounts for the user's state on the named DEX, so collateral shared across DEXes (e.g. through user abstraction) is not included, and\nliquidationPx\nis always\nnull\n. The first line of each file, and the first line written after the node starts, is a snapshot. Other lines contain diffs for users whose clearinghouse state changed, ignoring\ntime\n: every such user on an oracle update for the venue, or only the users involved in a fill, liquidation, funding payment, or transfer into or out of the venue.\nHIP-3* (testnet-only)\nStar actions\nA HIP-3 venue can be designated HIP-3* at time of creation by setting\nisStar: true\nin\nPerpDexSchemaInput\n. This optional boolean defaults to\nfalse\n. HIP-3* enables several features on top of the HIP-3 spec, including an allow-list and proxied user actions.\nOperations\nproxy\n\u2014 a pair of a user address and one proxy operation applied to that user.\nsetOracle\n\u2014 updates oracle prices, as described under Oracle below.\nProxy operations\nmodifyApproval\n\u2014\n{ \"modifyApproval\": true }\nadds the user to the allowlist,\nfalse\nremoves them and clears their flags. Removing a user who is not approved is a no-op. Re-approving an approved user is a no-op and keeps the user's flags. A removed user who is approved again starts with default flags.\nmodifyBackstopLiquidatorApproval\n\u2014\n{ \"modifyBackstopLiquidatorApproval\": true }\nallows the user to deposit into and withdraw from the venue's backstop liquidator via\nhip3LiquidatorTransfer\n;\nfalse\nrevokes this. The user must already be approved via\nmodifyApproval\n.\nsetReduceOnly\n\u2014\n{ \"setReduceOnly\": true }\nrestricts an approved user to reducing their positions on the venue. The user can only place reduce-only orders and TWAPs, modify orders into reduce-only orders, and cancel.\n{ \"setReduceOnly\": false }\nrestores full trading.\ncancel\n\u2014\n{ \"cancel\": { \"cancels\": [{ \"a\": <asset>, \"o\": <oid> }] } }\n, the standard\ncancel\nexchange-action payload. Cancels the user's resting orders by oid.\ncancelAll\n\u2014\n{ \"cancelAll\": { \"assets\": null } }\ncancels all of the user's resting orders and TWAPs on this venue.\n{ \"cancelAll\": { \"assets\": [<asset>, ...] } }\ncancels only the user's resting orders and TWAPs for the listed assets. The list must contain 1-10 entries.\norder\n\u2014\n{ \"order\": { \"orders\": [...], \"grouping\": \"na\" } }\n, the standard\norder\nexchange-action payload. Every order must be reduce-only (\n\"r\": true\n).\nsendAsset\n\u2014\n{ \"sendAsset\": { \"destination\": \"0xUSER_C\", \"amount\": \"100.0\" } }\n. Moves collateral from the proxied user's account on the DEX to\ndestination\n's account on the same venue.\nOracle\nHIP-3* venues update prices with the\nsetOracle\nstar operation deploy action.\noraclePxs\nis a list (sorted by key) of asset and spot oracle prices. External perp prices and mark prices are derived onchain based on the deployer's spot oracle price inputs.\nExternal perp prices\nare derived from the main dex asset with the same name, with the denomination converted to USDC. For example,\ntest:BTC\nuses the external perp price of\nBTC\n.\nMark prices\nare computed onchain as the median of:\nthe oracle price adjusted by an exponential moving average of the mid price's premium over the oracle price,\nthe external perp price, and\nthe local mark price (median(best bid, best ask, last trade price)).\nIf only two of these prices are available, an exponential moving average of the local mark price is added as a third input.\nPermissions\nFor HIP-3*,\nSubDeployerInput.variant\nalso accepts the objects shown below.\nOperation\nDeployer\nSub-deployer grant\nmodifyApproval\nyes\n{ \"hip3Star\": \"modifyApproval\" }\nmodifyBackstopLiquidatorApproval\nyes\n{ \"hip3Star\": \"modifyBackstopLiquidatorApproval\" }\nsetReduceOnly\nyes\n{ \"hip3Star\": \"setReduceOnly\" }\ncancel\nyes\n{ \"hip3Star\": \"cancel\" }\ncancelAll\nyes\n{ \"hip3Star\": \"cancelAll\" }\norder\nyes\n{ \"hip3Star\": \"order\" }\nsendAsset\nyes\n{ \"hip3Star\": \"sendAsset\" }\nsetOracle\nyes\n{ \"hip3Star\": \"setOracle\" }\nEach grant only covers its own operation. For example, the\nmodifyApproval\ngrant does not allow\nmodifyBackstopLiquidatorApproval\n,\nsetReduceOnly\n, or\nsendAsset\n. Likewise, the regular\n\"setOracle\"\ngrant, including the one given to\noracleUpdater\n, does not allow the\nsetOracle\nstar operation.\nUser star state\nThe\nuserStarState\ninfo request returns a user's approval state on every HIP-3* venue where the user has been approved. The state is\nnull\non venues that have since removed the user's approval.\nPrevious\nDeploying HIP-1 and HIP-2 assets\nNext\nHIP-4 deployer actions\nLast updated\n1 month ago"

    "https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/for-developers/api/hip-4-deployer-actions": {
      "title": "HIP-4 deployer actions",
      "hash": "076ecf19f45e2b8d02441317e7e473e0e9192c1a3f3b00afa1ff479c3922a420",
      "last_checked": "2026-11-19T08:53:37.904469",
      "content": "Overview\nTo ensure markets are high quality and well-defined, validators vote on outcome templates, which HIP-4 deployers use as the basis for permissionless deployments. Templates fix the structure of the specification's display text, side names, and the set of typed keywords.\nDeploying has no gas cost. Capacity is instead bounded by per-deployer limits (see Limits).\nAction format\nThe following actions are involved in deployment:\nCopy\n{\n\"\ntype\n\"\n:\n\"\nactivateOutcomeDeployer\n\"\n,\n\"\nactivate\n\"\n:\n{\n\"\nvenueName\n\"\n:\n<venue>\n}\n}\n{\n\"\ntype\n\"\n:\n\"\nactivateOutcomeDeployer\n\"\n,\n\"\ndeactivate\n\"\n:\nnull\n}\n{\n\"\ntype\n\"\n:\n\"\noutcomeDeploy\n\"\n,\n\"\nvenue\n\"\n:\n<venue>\n,\n\"\noperation\n\"\n:\n{\n\"\n<variant>\n\"\n:\n{\n...variant\nfields...\n}\n}\n}\nOutcomes and questions are referenced by the numeric index assigned at creation, e.g.\n\"outcome\": 7\n.\nVenue\n: every\noutcomeDeploy\naction carries a required top-level\nvenue\nfield, which must be the deployer's venue name (similar to the\ndex\nfield on HIP-3 deployer actions). Sub-deployer actions pass the venue of the deployer they act for, and settlements must target outcomes and questions of that venue's deployer.\nVenue name\n: 2\u20134 lowercase ASCII letters, subject to the same rules as HIP-3 perp DEX names. The name must be unique across the venue names of all deployers \u2014 including deactivated ones, whose names stay reserved \u2014 and must not match an existing perp DEX name (nor\nspot\n). Conversely, a registered venue name cannot be claimed by a later perp DEX deployment.\nAs with HIP-3, all lists of tuples should be lexicographically sorted before signing\nkeywordToValue\nis a sorted list of tuples mapping each template keyword to its value. For example,\n[[\"expiry\",\"20260801-0600\"],[\"target\",\"100\"],[\"underlying\",\"ABC\"]]\n.\nName-and-description values are two-element arrays:\n[\"<name>\", \"<description>\"]\n.\nsideNames\narrays are\n[\"<YES side name>\", \"<NO side name>\"]\n.\nAmounts and settlement fractions are decimal strings (\n\"1\"\n,\n\"0.25\"\n).\nActivation\nactivateOutcomeDeployer\nis an enum with\nactivate\nand\ndeactivate\nvariants:\nStaking requirement\n: active deployers must maintain the staking requirement for as long as it remains an outcome deployer. Requirements stack with the deployer's other staking requirements. For example, stake that counts towards HIP-3 deployment does not double-count towards outcome deployment.\nDeployers must use Standard account abstraction.\nDeactivation requires that the minimum deployer staking duration (183 days) has elapsed and that the deployer has no active outcomes. Deactivation is permanent: the account cannot activate again, and its venue name stays reserved.\nTemplates\nEvery deployer-created market comes from a template. A template has one of three roles:\nStandalone outcome\n: deploys a single YES/NO market; the template fixes the side names.\nQuestion\n: deploys a question (the container).\nQuestion outcome\n: deploys one outcome of a question; each question-outcome template declares its parent question template, and instantiations are only accepted under that parent.\nA template fixes display name and description text containing\n{keyword}\nplaceholders (e.g.,\n\"{underlying} above {target} at {expiry}\"\n) together with a typed\nhint\nper keyword. An instantiation supplies exactly one value per keyword.\nTemplate metadata includes a\nsemanticRestriction\nfield, which broadly defines the intended semantics of the markets instantiated from the template. Markets that contradict their template's semantic restriction are considered malformed and slashable by validators.\nKeyword value formats by hint type:\nHint\nValue format\ndateTime\nUTC time\n%Y%m%d-%H%M\n, e.g.\n\"20260712-1830\"\n; must be within the next year\ndate\nUTC date\nYYYYMMDD\n, e.g.\n\"20260712\"\n(end of day); must be within the next year\nstring\nfree text\nshortString\nfree text of at most 10 characters\nhlPerp\ncoin name of an existing perp, e.g.\n\"ABC\"\nor\n\"test:ABC\"\nuInt\nnonnegative integer that fits in a u64, e.g.\n\"250\"\n(no sign or leading zeros)\nuDecimal\nnonnegative decimal, e.g.\n\"0.5\"\nor\n\"250\"\n(no sign, exponent, or leading/trailing zeros)\nValues are at most 100 characters and cannot contain\n{\n,\n}\n, or\n|\n(\n:\nis allowed, e.g. for HIP-3 coin names).\nThe interpolated display text (template text with the values substituted) is also bounded: names are at most 70 characters for standalone outcomes and questions and at most 25 characters for question outcomes, and standalone side names are at most 10 characters.\nThe onchain outcome descriptions are derived from the instantiation:\nName\n:\ntemplate:<template_id>\n, e.g.\ntemplate:aaa\n. The\ntemplate:\nprefix is reserved. Only template deployments can produce it. Names are at most 100 characters.\nDescription\n: the keyword-value pairs sorted by keyword and joined as\nkeyword:value|keyword:value\n, e.g.\nexpiry:20260801-0600|target:100|underlying:BTC\n. Descriptions are at most 2000 characters.\nSide names\n: for standalone outcomes,\ntemplate:\nplus the template's side names (e.g.\ntemplate:Over\n/\ntemplate:Under\n); question outcomes use the defaults\nYes\n/\nNo\n.\nQuestion fallback\n: named\ntemplate fallback\nwith description\nother\nand side names\nYes\n/\nNo\n.\nDeployer fee scale\nTemplate instances (\nregisterStandaloneOutcomeFromTemplate\nand the\nquestionTemplateInstance\nof\nregisterQuestionFromTemplate\n) carry a required\ndeployerFeeScale\n, a decimal string in\n[0, 10]\n(\nDeployer fee scale cannot be negative or greater than 10.\n).\nUsers trading the outcome's markets pay the base outcome trading fee rate times\nscale + max(scale, 1)\n: the deployer receives the\nscale\ncomponent and the protocol the rest. With\n\"0\"\n, users pay the base rate and the deployer receives nothing. Maker rebates are never paid on outcome markets.\nA question's scale applies uniformly to its fallback and every question outcome, including ones associated later; question outcome template instances carry no scale of their own.\nPer-outcome scales are returned in the\noutcomeMeta\ninfo request.\nExamples, expressed as multiples of the user's base outcome trading fee rate:\ndeployerFeeScale\nTotal user fee\nDeployer receives\nProtocol receives\n\"1\"\n2x\n1x\n1x\n\"3\"\n6x\n3x\n3x\n\"10\"\n20x\n10x\n10x\n\"0.5\"\n1.5x\n0.5x\n1x\n\"0.25\"\n1.25x\n0.25x\n1x\n\"0\"\n1x\n0x\n1x\nBuilder codes\nBuilder codes inherit spot behavior for sell orders. Builder codes can also be applied to buy orders for outcome orders, and are also charged in the quote token. These builder fees are charged by the protocol on a best-effort basis. Builder interfaces may validate sufficient quote token balance when computing the maximum order size.\nAction reference\nregisterStandaloneOutcomeFromTemplate\nDeploys a standalone YES/NO market from a standalone outcome template.\nregisterQuestionFromTemplate\nDeploys a question and its question outcomes in one action.\nEach question outcome template must declare the question template as its parent. The same question outcome template may be instantiated multiple times with different values.\nA question with N question outcomes registers N + 1 outcomes (the fallback is created automatically). All count toward the deployer's active-outcome cap.\nMore question outcomes can be added to the live question with\nregisterAndAssociateNamedOutcomeFromTemplate\n. Bounded to at most 100 question outcomes per question.\nregisterAndAssociateNamedOutcomeFromTemplate\nAdds one question outcome to a live question of the deployer.\nThe question must have been deployed from a template, and the question outcome template must declare the question's template as its parent.\nThe new outcome inherits the question's\ndeployerFeeScale\nand counts toward the deployer's active-outcome and daily caps.\nHolders of the question's fallback YES token receive an equal balance of the new outcome's YES token, so existing \"other\" positions keep their meaning.\nsettleOutcome\nSettles one outcome of the deployer.\nnameAndDescription\nand\nsideNames\nmust exactly match the outcome being settled.\nsettleFraction\nis a decimal in\n[0, 1]\n. Standalone outcomes may settle to any fraction (e.g.\n\"0.66\"\nfor scalar payouts); outcomes that belong to a question must settle to exactly\n\"0\"\nor\n\"1\"\n.\ndetails\nmust be empty.\nFor question outcomes, settlement is sequential: question outcomes may settle to\n\"0\"\nin any order. A single outcome settles to\n\"1\"\nafter it is the last active question outcome, which automatically settles the fallback to 0 and settles the question.\nsettleQuestion2\nSettles all remaining question outcomes of a question in one action. Note: The original\nsettleQuestion\nvariant is discontinued.\noutcomeSettlements\nmust cover exactly the question's remaining active question outcomes, with exactly one settling to\n\"1\"\nand all others to\n\"0\"\n. The fallback settles to 0 automatically.\nsetSubDeployers\nGrants or revokes sub-deployer permissions.\nA sub-deployer granted a variant can send that action on the deployer's behalf: registrations are created under the deployer's venue and count toward the deployer's limits, and settlements can only target the deployer's own outcomes and questions.\nvariant\nis one of\nregisterStandaloneOutcomeFromTemplate\n,\nregisterQuestionFromTemplate\n,\nregisterAndAssociateNamedOutcomeFromTemplate\n,\nsettleOutcome\n, or\nsettleQuestion\n. The\nsettleQuestion\ngrant authorizes the\nsettleQuestion2\naction.\nallowed\nadds or removes the user from the authorized set for the variant.\nRead API\n{\"type\": \"outcomeMeta\"}\ninfo request includes non-null outcome deployers.\n{\"type\": \"outcomeTemplates\"}\ninfo request returns all templates.\nLimits\nAt most\nN\nactive outcomes per deployer (N=10 on testnet). Settling outcomes frees capacity.\nA deployer can deploy\nM\noutcomes per day (M=50 on testnet).\nPrevious\nHIP-3 deployer actions\nNext\nHyperEVM\nLast updated\n2 months ago"

    "https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/for-developers/api/nonces-and-api-wallets": {
      "title": "Nonces and API wallets",
      "hash": "3725fec447d838a05a82c2a44ac2cc88408d38b804283c6ea9b336edd95c80e8",
      "last_checked": "2026-11-19T08:53:40.749576",
      "content": "Background\nA decentralized L1 must prevent replay attacks. When a user signs a USDC transfer transaction, the receiver cannot broadcast it multiple times to drain the sender's wallet. To solve this Ethereum stores a \"nonce\" for each address, which is a number that starts at 0. Each transaction must use exactly \"nonce + 1\" to be included.\nAPI wallets\nThese are also known as\nagent wallets\nin the docs. A master account can approve API wallets to sign on behalf of the master account or any of the sub-accounts.\nNote that API wallets are only used to sign. To query the account data associated with a master or sub-account, you must pass in the actual address of that account. A common pitfall is to use the agent wallet which leads to an empty result.\nAPI wallet pruning\nAPI wallets and their associated nonce state may be pruned in the following cases:\nThe wallet is deregistered. This happens to an existing unnamed API Wallet when an ApproveAgent action is sent to register a new unnamed API Wallet. This also happens to an existing named API Wallet when an ApproveAgent action is sent with a matching name.\nThe wallet expires.\nThe account that registered the agent no longer has funds.\nImportant:\nfor those using API wallets programmatically, it is\nstrongly\nsuggested to not reuse their addresses. Once an agent is deregistered, its used nonce state may be pruned. Generate a new agent wallet on future use to avoid unexpected behavior. For example, previously signed actions can be replayed once the nonce set is pruned.\nHyperliquid nonces\nEthereum's design does not work for an onchain order book. A market making strategy can send thousands of orders and cancels in a second. Requiring a precise ordering of inclusion on the blockchain will break any strategy.\nOn Hyperliquid, the 100 highest nonces are stored per address. Every new transaction must have nonce larger than the smallest nonce in this set and also never have been used before. Nonces are tracked per signer, which is the user address if signed with private key of the address, or the agent address if signed with an API wallet.\nNonces must be within\n(T - 2 days, T + 1 day)\n, where\nT\nis the unix millisecond timestamp on the block of the transaction.\nThe following steps may help port over an automated strategy from a centralized exchange:\nUse a API wallet per trading process. Note that nonces are stored per signer (i.e. private key), so separate subaccounts signed by the same API wallet will share the nonce tracker of the API wallet. It's recommended to use separate API wallets for different subaccounts.\nIn each trading process, have a task that periodically batches order and cancel requests every 0.1 seconds. It is recommended to batch IOC and GTC orders separately from ALO orders because ALO order-only batches are prioritized by the validators.\nThe trading logic tasks send orders and cancels to the batching task.\nFor each batch of orders or cancels, fetch and increment an atomic counter that ensures a unique nonce for the address. The atomic counter can be fast-forwarded to current unix milliseconds if needed.\nThis structure is robust to out-of-order transactions within 2 seconds, which should be sufficient for an automated strategy geographically near an API server.\nSuggestions for subaccount and vault users\nNote that nonces are stored per signer, which is the address of the private key used to sign the transaction. Therefore, it's recommended that each trading process or frontend session use a separate private key for signing. In particular, a single API wallet signing for a user, vault, or subaccount all share the same nonce set.\nIf users want to use multiple subaccounts in parallel, it would easier to generate two separate API wallets under the master account, and use one API wallet for each subaccount. This avoids collisions between the nonce set used by each subaccount.\nPrevious\nTick and lot size\nNext\nInfo endpoint\nLast updated\n1 year ago"
    },
    "https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/for-developers/api/notation": {
      "title": "Notation",
      "hash": "9293fff186a52dbe147926b16a7771bd51542a950aa3dd8376bde382c12a6f5e",
      "last_checked": "2026-11-19T08:53:41.908719",
      "content": "The current v0 API currently uses some nonstandard notation. Relevant standardization will be batched into a breaking v1 API change.\nAbbreviation\nFull name\nExplanation\nPx\nPrice\nSz\nSize\nIn units of coin, i.e. base currency\nSzi\nSigned size\nPositive for long, negative for short\nNtl\nNotional\nUSD amount, Px * Sz\nSide\nSide of trade or book\nB = Bid = Buy, A = Ask = Short.     "https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/for-developers/api/optimizing-latency": {
      "title": "Optimizing latency",
      "hash": "5443b19160b553dc799a484dee197a15831b84150469262fb992298c6cda648d",
      "last_checked": "2026-11-19T08:53:43.130371",
      "content": "The suggestions below are for informational purposes only and a non-comprehensive starting point. They are not trading advice and do not guarantee any latency or trading outcome.\nOptimizing read latency (receiving market data)\nRun a non-validating node against a reliable peer, such as Hyper Foundation non-validator.\nRun node with\n--disable-output-file-buffering\nto get outputs as soon as blocks are executed\nRun node with sufficient machines specs, at least 32 logical cores, 128 GB RAM, and 500 MB/s disk throughput. Increasing cores can reduce latency because blocks will be faster to execute.\nConstruct book and other exchange state locally using outputs from node, which has faster and more granular data than the API. See\nhttps://github.com/hyperliquid-dex/order_book_server\nfor an example on how to build an order book on the same machine that is running a node. This repo is not actively maintained. It is recommended to fork the repo or write an implementation from scratch to incorporate details such as ALO priority fees or other edge cases.\n--batch-by-block\non the node will wait until the end of the block to write the data. The example order book server above uses this to simplify logic, but a further optimization could include turning the flag off and inferring block boundaries otherwise.\nLatency-sensitive users may find\nsplit_client_blocks: true\nuseful, as it streams transactions to be committed before they are included in client blocks. This can lead to 70-150ms improvement in latency when reading inputs. As documented in the node repository, these are the fastest transaction inputs to execution, broadcast even before they are executed by the L1. The tradeoff\u00a0with the faster input data is that the results from execution are not yet available. However, with simple actions like orders, the consumer can stream estimated account values for all users to predict execution results with reasonable accuracy.\nThe foundation non-validator is one of the highest uptime and low latency non-validator peers. See\nhere\nfor details on access for latency-sensitive users.\nRead priority fees are the fastest way to read raw data from the network. See\nhere\nfor more details.\nOptimizing write latency (sending orders and cancels)\nCancels should be sent with the\nfast\nflag. There is no disadvantage to the fast flag, except that fast cancels cannot be used to cancel trigger orders.\nWrite priority fees are the fastest way to send orders to the network. See\nhere\nfor more details.\nHyperliquid's execution logic is designed to protect makers against toxic flow, so that end users see tight spreads and deep liquidity even during volatility. The primary driver of this effect is that cancels and ALO orders sent at time\nt\nwill almost always execute before IOC and GTC orders sent at time\nt\n. This prioritization spans several blocks. The secondary effect is that each L1 block contains only a few consensus bundles. Within each bundle, ALO and cancels are executed before other transactions.\nEnd-to-end latency on Hyperliquid is not apples-to-apples with centralized matching engines, because the order book is fully onchain. End-to-end latency includes time to API server, mempool inclusion time, and time to commit (usually 2 blocks in pipelined HyperBFT consensus). The sequencing of transactions has far less noise than the end-to-end latency suggests. While a cancel or ALO order can show ~380ms end-to-end latency, users should be able to run experiments with <10ms variation between send times and see predictable ordering on the L1.\nUsers who care to reduce the end-to-end write latency (for example to predict the sequence of transactions that will be committed) can read them from split client blocks in the non-validating node (see read latency section above). This approach does not include the execution result of the transaction.\nMempool prioritization has restrictions on the actions sent.\nMempool prioritization will only work for users in Standard abstraction. As a reminder, latency sensitive users should not use abstractions such as Unified Account and Portfolio Margin.\nActions other than\nnoop, scheduledCancel, cancel, cancelByCloid, order, modify, batchModify\nsent on HyperCore will cause all actions to be deprioritized while that action has not yet been committed. This holds across sub-accounts associated with and vaults led by the same master user which originate the non-trading action sent. It does not affect other subaccounts of vaults of the same master account.\nUsers who have ever directly sent CoreWriter actions will be deprioritized in the mempool. This does not affect the vast majority of use cases of CoreWriter, which go through smart contracts rather than the user itself.\nIOC and ALO priority fees require a minimum delegatable balance of 10 HYPE. The priority fee charged remains a function of only the executed size.\nPrevious\nActivation gas fee\nNext\nPriority fees\nLast updated\n7 days ago"
