    "https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/funding": {
      "title": "Funding",
      "hash": "01bb7dd6afb8c0506b6f60493cde89c23e5c7e6b034b1d082132c9d439029b24",
      "last_checked": "2026-11-19T08:54:22.165381",
      "content": "Overview\nFunding rates for crypto perpetual contracts are a mechanism that is used to ensure the price of the contract stays close to the underlying asset's price.\nThe funding rate is a periodic fee that is paid by one side of the contract (either long or short) to the other side. Funding is purely peer-to-peer and no fees are collected on the payments.\nThe rate is calculated based on the difference between the contract's price and the spot price of the underlying asset. For consistency with CEXs, interest rate component is predetermined at 0.01% every 8 hours, which is 0.00125% every hour, or 11.6% APR paid to short. This represents the difference in cost to borrow USD versus spot crypto.\nThe premium component fluctuates based on the difference between the perpetual contract's price and the underlying spot oracle price. If the contract's price is higher than the oracle price, the premium and hence the funding rate will be positive, and the long position will pay the short position. Conversely, if the contract's price is lower than the spot price, the funding rate will be negative, and the short position will pay the long position.\nThe funding rate on Hyperliquid is paid every hour. The funding rate is added or subtracted from the balance of contract holders at the funding interval.\nFunding rates are designed to prevent large price disparities between the perpetual contract and the underlying asset. When the funding rate is high, it can incentivize traders to take the opposite position and help to bring the contract's price closer to the spot price of the underlying asset.\nTechnical details\nFunding on Hyperliquid is designed to closely match the process used by centralized perpetual exchanges.\nThe funding rate formula applies to 8 hour funding rate. However, funding is paid every hour at one eighth of the computed rate for each hour.\nThe specific formula is\nFunding Rate (F) = Average Premium Index (P) + clamp (interest rate - Premium Index (P), -0.0005, 0.0005)\n. The premium is sampled every 5 seconds and averaged over the hour.\nAs described in the\nclearinghouse\nsection, the oracle prices are computed by each validator as the weighted median of CEX spot prices for each asset, with weights depending on the liquidity of the CEX.\npremium = impact_price_difference / oracle_price\nwhere\nimpact_price_difference = max(impact_bid_px - oracle_px, 0) - max(oracle_px - impact_ask_px, 0)\n.\nand\nimpact_bid_px\nand\nimpact_ask_px\nare the average execution prices to trade\nimpact_notional_usd\non the bid and ask sides, respectively. See the contract specifications for the impact notional used, as well as other contract specific parameters.\nFor HIP-3 perps, a more responsive premium formula is used to allow deployers to express a larger range of funding behaviors using the funding rate multiplier and interest rate:\npremium = (0.5 * (impact_bid_px + impact_ask_px) / oracle_px) - 1\n.\nFunding on Hyperliquid is capped at 0.5%/hour. Note that this is much less aggressive capping than CEX counterparts. The funding cap and funding interval do not depend on the asset.\nNote that the funding payment at the end of the interval is\nposition_size * oracle_price * funding_rate\n. In particular, the spot oracle price is used to convert the position size to notional value,\nnot the mark price.\nNumerical Example\nHere is an explicit example computation:\nThe interest rate is 0.01% (fixed).\nThe perpetual contract is trading at a premium, with the impact bid price being $10,100, and the spot price at $10,000.\nThe premium index is calculated as the difference between the two prices, which is $100 in this case.\nThe funding interval is 1 hour.\nYou hold a long position of 10 contracts, each representing 1 BTC.\nFirst, calculate the premium:\nPremium = (Impact bid price - Spot Price) / Spot Price = ($10,100 - $10,000) / $10,000 Premium = 0.01 (or 1%)\nNext, clamp the interest rate minus the premium rate at 0.05%:\nClamped Difference = min(max(Interest Rate - Premium Rate, -0.05%), 0.05%)\nClamped Difference = min(max(0.01% - 1%, -0.05%), 0.05%)\nClamped Difference = min(max(-0.99%, -0.05%), 0.05%) Clamped Difference = -0.05%\nNow, calculate the funding rate:\nFunding Rate = Premium Rate + Clamped Difference Funding Rate = 1% + (-0.05%)\nFunding Rate = 0.95%\nPrevious\nAuto-deleveraging\nNext\nOrder book\nLast updated\n18 days ago"
    },
    "https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/hyperps": {
      "title": "Hyperps",
      "hash": "3f0c8c95391b40a73f89294dfeb912a8197e495f1a4e33e7c47ced228900f23e",
      "last_checked": "2026-11-19T08:54:23.235521",
      "content": "High level summary\nHyperps\n(Hyperliquid-only perps) trade like vanilla perpetual contracts, but do not require an underlying spot or index oracle price. Instead, the funding rate is determined relative to a moving average hyperp mark price in place of the usual spot price. This makes the hyperp price more stable and less susceptible to manipulation, unlike usual pre-launch futures.\nThis new derivative design does not require an underlying asset or index that exists at all points of the hyperp's lifetime, only that the underlying asset or index eventually exists for settlement or conversion.\nWhen trading hyperps, funding rates are very important to consider. If there is heavy price momentum in one direction, funding will heavily incentivize positions in the opposite direction for the next eight hours. As always, be sure to understand the contract before trading.\nConversion to vanilla perps\nFor a hyperp tracking ABC, shortly after when ABC/USDT is listed on Binance, OKX, or Bybit spot trading, ABC-USD will convert to a normal ABC-USD perp.\nHyperp mechanism details\nHyperps work just like normal perpetual contracts, except the external spot/index oracle price is replaced with an 8 hour exponentially weighted moving average of the last day's minutely mark prices.\nPrecisely,\noracle_price(t) = min[sum_{i=0}^1439 [(t - i minutes < t_list ? initial_mark_price : mark_price(t - i minutes)) * exp(-i/480)] * (1 - exp(-1/480)) / (1 - exp(-3)), intial_mark_price * 4]\nHere\na ? b : c\nevaluates to\nb\nif\na\nis true and otherwise\nc.\nSamples are taken on the first block after each unix minute, but the timestamps used are the nearest exact minute multiples. When there are fewer than 480 mark price samples, the initial mark price is used as the padding value.\nFunding rate premium samples are computed as 1% of the usual clamped interest rate and premium formula. See Funding docs for more details.\nThe mark price of Hyperps incorporate the weighted median of pre-launch perp prices from CEXs as a component in the usual mark price formula. Despite the often significantly different contract specifications between hyperps and other venues' pre-launch perp markets, they are nonetheless included as mark price inputs to provide greater mark price stability during volatility.\nThe mark price of hyperps are capped at 3x the 8-hour mark price EMA. Hyperps with external prelaunch perp listings have mark price capped to 1.5x the median external perp price (the third component of the mark price). The oracle price is also restricted to be at most 4 times the one month average mark price as an additional safeguard against manipulation.\nPrevious\nSelf-trade prevention\nNext\nDelisting\nLast updated\n3 months ago"
    },
    "https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/liquidations": {
      "title": "Liquidations",
      "hash": "39e2f54077aed6758012982ee609d66b71eac0e57cd45345ee2b3208b244cf72",
      "last_checked": "2026-11-19T08:54:24.280326",
      "content": "Overview\nA liquidation event occurs when a trader's positions move against them to the point where the account equity falls below the maintenance margin. The maintenance margin is half of the initial margin at max leverage, which varies from 3-40x. In other words, the maintenance margin is between 1.25% (for 40x max leverage assets) and 16.7% (for 3x max leverage assets) depending on the asset.\nWhen the account equity drops below maintenance margin, the positions are first attempted to be entirely closed by sending market orders to the book. The orders are for the full size of the position, and may be fully or partially closed. If the positions are entirely or partially closed such that the maintenance margin requirements are met, any remaining collateral remains with the trader.\nIf the account equity drops below 2/3 of the maintenance margin without successful liquidation through the book, a backstop liquidation happens through the liquidator vault. See Liquidator Vault explanation below for more details.\nWhen a cross position is backstop liquidated, the trader's cross positions and cross margin are all transferred to the liquidator. In particular, if the trader has no isolated positions, the trader ends up with zero account equity.\nWhen an isolated position is backstop liquidated, that isolated position and isolated margin are transferred to the liquidator. The user's cross margin and positions are untouched.\nDuring backstop liquidation, the maintenance margin is not returned to the user. This is because the liquidator vault requires a buffer to make sure backstop liquidations are profitable on average. In order to avoid losing the maintenance margin, traders can place stop loss orders or exit the positions before the mark price reaches the liquidation price.\nLiquidations use the mark price, which combines external CEX prices with Hyperliquid's book state. This makes liquidations more robust than using a single instantaneous book price. During times of high volatility or on highly leveraged positions, mark price may be significantly different from book price. It is recommended to use the exact formula for precise monitoring of liquidations.\nMotivation\nAs described above, the majority of liquidations on Hyperliquid are sent directly to the order book. This allows all users to compete for the liquidation flow, and allows the liquidated user to keep any remaining margin. Unlike CEXs there is no clearance fee on liquidations.\nThe resulting system is transparent and prioritizes retaining as much capital as possible for the liquidated user.\nPartial Liquidations\nFor liquidatable positions larger than 100k USDC (10k USDC on testnet for easier testing), only 20% of the position will be sent as a market liquidation order to the book. After a block where any position of a user is partially liquidated, there is a cooldown period of 30 seconds. During this cooldown period, all market liquidation orders for that user will be for the entire position.\nLiquidator Vault\nBackstop liquidations on Hyperliquid are democratized through the liquidator vault, which is a component strategy of HLP. Positions that are below 2/3 of the maintenance margin can be taken over by the liquidator vault.\nOn average, backstop liquidations are profitable for the liquidator. On most venues, this profit goes to the exchange operator or privileged market makers who internalize the flow. On Hyperliquid, the pnl stream from liquidations go entirely to the community through HLP.\nComputing Liquidation Price\nWhen entering a trade, an estimated liquidation price is shown. This estimation may be inaccurate compared to the position's estimated liquidation price due to changing liquidity on the book.\nOnce a position is opened, a liquidation price is shown. This price has the certainty of the entry price, but still may not be the actual liquidation price due to funding payments or changes in unrealized pnl in other positions (for cross margin positions).\nThe actual liquidation price is independent on the leverage set for cross margin positions. A cross margin position at lower leverage simply uses more collateral.\nThe liquidation price does depend on leverage set for isolated margin positions, because the amount of isolated margin allocated depends on the initial margin set.\nWhen there is insufficient margin to make the trade, the liquidation price estimate assumes that the account is topped up to the initial margin requirement.\nThe precise formula for the liquidation price of a position is\nliq_price = price - side * margin_available / position_size / (1 - l * side)\nwhere\nl = 1 / MAINTENANCE_LEVERAGE\n. For assets with margin tiers, maintenance leverage depends on the unique margin tier corresponding to the position value at the liquidation price.\nside = 1 for long and -1 for short\nmargin_available (cross) = account_value - maintenance_margin_required\nmargin_available (isolated) = isolated_margin - maintenance_margin_required\nPrevious\nRobust price indices\nNext\nAuto-deleveraging\nLast updated\n1 year ago"
    },
    "https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/margin-tiers": {
      "title": "Margin tiers",
      "hash": "aa2b8033e7bf724848e46180426a246bffa7e236ea99c0b1ae51b8a615df1b81",
      "last_checked": "2026-11-19T08:54:25.358934",
      "content": "Like most centralized exchanges, the tiered leverage formula on Hyperliquid is as follows:\nmaintenance_margin = notional_position_value * maintenance_margin_rate - maintenance_deduction\nOn Hyperliquid,\nmaintenance_margin_rate\nand\nmaintenance_deduction\ndepend only on the margin tiers, not the asset.\nmaintenance_margin_rate(tier = n) = (Initial Margin Rate at Maximum leverage at tier n) / 2\n. For example, at 20x max leverage,\nmaintenance_margin_rate = 2.5%\n.\nMaintenance deduction is defined at each tier to account for the different maintenance margin rates used at previous tiers:\nmaintenance_deduction(tier = 0) = 0\nmaintenance_deduction(tier = n) = maintenance_deduction(tier = n - 1) + notional_position_lower_bound(tier = n) * (maintenance_margin_rate(tier = n) - maintenance_margin_rate(tier = n - 1))\nfor\nn > 0\nIn other words, maintenance deduction is defined so that new positions opened at each tier increase maintenance margin at\nmaintenance_margin_rate\n, while having the total maintenance margin be a continuous function of position size.\nMargin tables have unique IDs and the tiers can be found in the\nmeta\nInfo response. For IDs less than 50, there is a single tier with max leverage equal to the ID.\nMainnet Margin Tiers\nMainnet margin tiers are enabled for the assets below:\nBTC\nNotional Position Value (USDC)\nMax Leverage\n0-150M\n40\n>150M\n20\nETH\nNotional Position Value (USDC)\nMax Leverage\n0-100M\n25\n>100M\n15\nSOL\nNotional Position Value (USDC)\nMax Leverage\n0-70M\n20\n>70M\n10\nXRP\nNotional Position Value (USDC)\nMax Leverage\n0-40M\n20\n>40M\n10\nAAVE, ADA, APT, AVAX, BCH, CRV, DOGE, ENA, FARTCOIN, HYPE, kBONK, kPEPE, LINK, LTC, NEAR, PUMP, SUI, TRUMP, UNI, WLD, ZEC\nNotional Position Value (USDC)\nMax Leverage\n0-20M\n10\n>20M\n5\nARB, BNB, DOT, JUP, kSHIB, MKR, ONDO, PAXG, TON, TRX, XPL\nNotional Position Value (USDC)\nMax Leverage\n0-3M\n10\n>3M\n5\nTestnet Margin Tiers\nThe tiers on testnet are lower than mainnet, for ease of testing.\nARB, ATOM, AVAX, FARTCOIN, ICP, TAO - testnet only\nNotional Position Value (USDC)\nMax Leverage\n0-10k\n10\n>10k\n5\nDOGE, TIA, SUI, kSHIB, AAVE, TON - testnet only\nNotional Position Value (USDC)\nMax Leverage\n0-20k\n10\n>20k\n5\nETH - testnet only\nNotional Position Value (USDC)\nMax Leverage\n0-20k\n25\n20-50k\n10\n>50k\n5\nBTC - testnet only\nNotional Position Value (USDC)\nMax Leverage\n0-10k\n40\n10-50k\n25\n>50k\n10\nPrevious\nPortfolio margin\nNext\nRobust price indices\nLast updated\n6 months ago"

    "https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/margining": {
      "title": "Margining",
      "hash": "1f22d45e1d9d111bdb08516976b4483f8c070e0a9946a27033d65b69ce8d4813",
      "last_checked": "2026-11-19T08:54:26.440723",
      "content": "Margin computations follow similar formulas to major centralized derivatives exchanges.\nMargin Mode\nWhen opening a position, a margin mode is selected.\nCross margin\nis the default, which allows for maximal capital efficiency by sharing collateral between all other cross margin positions.\nIsolated margin\nis also supported, which allows an asset's collateral to be constrained to that asset. Liquidations in that asset do not affect other isolated positions or cross positions. Similarly, cross liquidations or other isolated liquidations do not affect the original isolated position.\nSome assets are\nstrict isolated\n, which functions the same as isolated margin with the additional constraint that margin cannot be removed. Margin is proportionally removed as the position is closed.\nHIP-3 Margin Modes\nWhen users have perp positions across multiple DEXs, cross margin behaves different depending on the user's account abstraction. For unified account and portfolio margin, the user's cross margin positions in DEXs with the same collateral all share margin. For standard abstraction, cross margin only applies to the assets within the same DEX. See\nhere\nfor more details.\nHIP-3 DEXs also support \"no cross\" margin mode, which allows isolated margin with margin removal enabled, but does not allow cross margin.\nInitial Margin and Leverage\nLeverage can be set by a user to any integer between 1 and the max leverage. Max leverage depends on the asset.\nThe margin required to open a position is\nposition_size * mark_price / leverage\n. The initial margin is used by the position and cannot be withdrawn for cross margin positions. Isolated positions support adding and removing margin after opening the position. Unrealized pnl for cross margin positions will automatically be available as initial margin for new positions, while isolated positions will apply unrealized pnl as additional margin for the open position.\nThe leverage of an existing position can be increased without closing the position. Leverage is only checked upon opening a position. Afterwards, the user is responsible for monitoring the leverage usage to avoid liquidation. Possible actions to take on positions with negative unrealized pnl include partially or fully closing the position, adding margin (if isolated), and depositing USDC (if cross).\nUnrealized PNL and transfer margin requirements\nUnrealized pnl can be withdrawn from isolated positions or cross account, but only if the remaining margin is at least 10% of the total notional position value of all open positions. The margin remaining must also meet the initial margin requirement, i.e.\ntransfer_margin_required = max(initial_margin_required, 0.1 * total_position_value)\nHere, \"transferring\" includes any action that removes margin from a position, other than trading. Examples include withdrawals, transfer to spot wallet, and isolated margin transfers.\nMaintenance Margin and Liquidations\nCross positions are liquidated when the account value (including unrealized pnl) is less than the\nmaintenance margin\ntimes the total open notional position. The maintenance margin is currently set to half of the initial margin at max leverage.\nIsolated positions are liquidated by the same maintenance margin logic, but the only inputs to the computation are the isolated margin and the notional value of the isolated position.\nPrevious\nContract specifications\nNext\nAccount abstraction modes\nLast updated\n8 months ago"
    },

Users who care about the precise formula can get their account value and pnl history and compute it however they choose.\nThe formula used on the frontend is the maximum over times\nend > start\nof the value\n(pnl(end) - pnl(start)) / account_value(start)\nNote that the denominator is account value and the numerator is pnl.     "https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/order-types": {
      "title": "Order types",
      "hash": "af04730a1b522620c3da47a7c60701832438c7c830381c8cf917142e6f9d23b2",
      "last_checked": "2026-11-19T08:54:30.722392",
      "content": "Order types\nMarket: An order that executes immediately at the current market price\nLimit: An order that executes at the selected limit price or better\nStop Market: A market order that is activated when the price reaches the selected trigger price. For long orders, the trigger price needs to be higher than the mid price. For short orders, the trigger price needs to be lower than the mid price\nStop Limit: A limit order that is activated when the price reaches the selected trigger price\nTake Market:  A market order that is activated when the price reaches the selected trigger price. For long orders, the trigger price needs to be lower than the mid price. For short orders, the trigger price needs to be higher than the mid price\nTake Limit: A limit order that is activated when the price reaches the selected trigger price\nChase: A Post Only (ALO) limit order that automatically re-prices to track the best bid or ask until it is filled or terminated. The order rests one tick above the best bid (for buys) or one tick below the best ask (for sells), or at the best bid/ask when the spread is a single tick. Chase orders run in the browser tab where they are created, and up to 5 can be active at once\nScale: Multiple limit orders in a set price range\nTrailing Stop: A market order that is activated when the mark price retraces from its best level by the selected distance or percentage. For trailing stops that close long positions, or open shorts, the trigger price follows the highest mark price reached since activation. For trailing stops that close short position, or open longs, it follows the lowest. The trigger price never moves in the opposite direction. Trailing stops are available for perp markets\nTWAP: A large order divided into smaller suborders and executed at regular intervals, based on order size and running time inputs. Intervals are a minimum of 30 seconds. TWAP suborders have a maximum slippage of 3%\nRandomize: If enabled, the size of each sub-order will be adjusted randomly up to \u00b120% of the original trade size\nTrigger Price: The TWAP order will be activated when the mark price reaches the trigger price set\nMax/Min Price: Acts as a limit price for the TWAP. Suborders will not fill at a price worse than this value (above the max price for buys/longs, below the min price for sells/shorts).\nTWAP details\nDuring execution, a TWAP order attempts to meet an execution target which is defined as the elapsed time divided by the total time times the total size. Suborders are sent at a fixed interval, calculated from the total size and running time inputs. Larger orders over shorter durations are split into suborders sent as often as every 30 seconds; smaller orders over longer durations are split into suborders spaced further apart. Running time can be set from 5 minutes to 7 days, with a $100 minimum total order size.\nExample:\nA $10,000 order over 1 hour is split into ~121 suborders of ~$83, sent every 30 seconds\nA $10,000 order over 4 days is split into ~1,000 suborders of ~$10, sent roughly every 6 minutes\nA suborder is constrained to have a max slippage of 3%. When suborders do not fully fill because of market conditions (e.g., price is worse than max/min price set, wide spread, low liquidity, etc.), the TWAP may fall behind its execution target. In this case, the TWAP will try to catch up to this execution target during later suborders. These later suborders will be larger but subject to the constraint of 3 times the normal suborder size (defined as total TWAP size divided by number of suborders). It is possible that if too many suborders did not fill then the TWAP order may not fully catch up to the total size by the end. Like normal market orders, TWAP suborders do not fill during the post-only period of a network upgrade.\nOrder options\nReduce Only: An order that reduces a current position as opposed to opening a new position in the opposite direction\nGood Til Cancel (GTC): An order that rests on the order book until it is filled or canceled\nPost Only (ALO): An order that is added to the order book but doesn\u2019t execute immediately. It is only executed as a resting order\nImmediate or Cancel (IOC): An order that will be canceled if it is not immediately filled\nTake Profit: An order that triggers when the Take Profit (TP) price is reached.\nStop Loss: An order that triggers when the Stop Loss (SL) price is reached\nTP and SL orders are often used by traders to set targets and protect profits or minimize losses on positions. TP and SL are automatically market orders. You can set a limit price and configure the amount of the position to have a TP or SL\nPrevious\nOrder book\nNext\nTake profit and stop loss orders (TP/SL)\nLast updated\n18 days ago"
    },

    "https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/portfolio-margin": {
      "title": "Portfolio margin",
      "hash": "c1087f3ecf16033c6ce452e81884b446d56f25236d4b5b14e6d72cfa8d63ba68",
      "last_checked": "2026-11-19T08:54:33.952652",
      "content": "Under portfolio margin, a user\u2019s spot and perps trading are unified for greater capital efficiency. Furthermore, portfolio margin accounts automatically earn yield on all borrowable assets not actively used for trading.\nPortfolio margin unlocks functionality such as the carry trade where a spot balance is offset by a short perps position, collateralized by the spot balance. Spot and perp pnl offset each other, protecting against liquidation on the perp position. More generally, spot and perps trading can be performed from a single unified balance. For example, a user could also hold HYPE and immediately buy BTC on the BTC/USDC book. All HIP-3 DEXs are included in portfolio margin, though not all HIP-3 DEX collateral assets are borrowable. Future HyperCore asset classes and primitives will support portfolio margin as well.\nUsers can supply eligible quote assets to earn yield. This synergizes and composes with HyperEVM lending protocols. In a future upgrade, CoreWriter will expose the same supply action for smart contracts. Portfolio margin intentionally does not bring a full-fledged lending market to HyperCore, as that is best built by independent teams on the EVM. For example, HyperCore lending is not tokenized, but an EVM protocol could do so by launching a fully onchain yield-bearing ERC20 token contract through CoreWriter and precompiles. Portfolio margin introduces organic demand to borrow and should expand the value proposition of teams building on the HyperEVM.\nIMPORTANT: Portfolio margin is a complex technical upgrade and requires bootstrapping the supply side for borrowable assets. Portfolio margin accounts will fall back to non-portfolio margin behavior when caps are hit. The following requirements apply:\nMaster account >$5M in weighted volume or account value >$10k\nAccount value <$25M\nUSDT: 50M USDT global supply cap, 10M USDT global borrow cap, 5M USDT user supply cap, 1M USDT user borrow cap\nUSDC: 1B USDC global supply cap, 500M USDC global borrow cap, 250M USDC user supply cap, 50M USDC user borrow cap\nHYPE: 25M HYPE global supply cap, 1M HYPE user supply cap\nBTC: 5k BTC global supply cap, 200 BTC user supply cap\nLTV and borrowing\nUnder portfolio margin, eligible collateral assets have an LTV (loan-to-value) ratio between 0 and 1. HYPE has an LTV of 0.65 and BTC has an LTV of 0.5. When placing spot and perp orders under portfolio margin, insufficient balance will automatically borrowed against eligible collateral up to\ntoken_balance * borrow_oracle_price * ltv\n, where price is denominated in the asset being borrowed.\nBorrowed assets accrue interest continuously, and are indexed hourly to match the perp funding interval. Portfolio margin users pay interest on borrowed assets and earn interest on idle assets according to the same rate. The borrow interest rate for stablecoins is set at\n0.05 + 4.75 * max(0, utilization - 0.8)\nAPY, compounded continuously depending on the instantaneous value of\nutilization = total_borrowed_value / total_supplied_value\n. Earned interest is accrued proportionally to all suppliers. The protocol retains 10% of borrowed interest as a buffer for future liquidations.\nExample: Carry trade\nThe carry trade becomes significantly more capital efficient with portfolio margin, as there is no trading cost to rebalance over signifcant price ranges. A portfolio margin account's spot borrow and perps pnl offset each other for accounting. The trader still needs to account for external factors such as funding, interest, and drift between spot and perp prices.\nFor example, a user holds 1 BTC in spot and shorts 1 BTC-USDC perp at 10x leverage. If BTC's price is 100k, the user only pays interest on the 1/10 initial margin but earns funding on the full 100k position. If BTC's price moves down to 50k, the trader has unrealized pnl in USDC. The trader can choose to maintain the notional value of the trade by buying more spot BTC and increasing the perp short. If BTC's price moves up to 150k, portfolio margin automatically borrows 50k USDC against the spot BTC, now worth 150k. The user can sell BTC and close the perp position to maintain the notional exposure of the funding trade. If BTC's price moves up to 200k, the trader must reduce the notional exposure of the funding trade to avoid a borrow-lend liquidation.\nNote that the hedged price range increased dramatically compared to the same trade without portfolio margin, where the perp leg is collateralized by USDC.\nLiquidations\nPortfolio margin is a generalization of cross margin. Instead of margining all perp positions within one DEX together, all cross margin perp positions and spot balances are collectively margined together within one account. Sub-accounts are still treated separately under portfolio margin.\nLiquidations are triggered when the entire portfolio margin account is below its portfolio maintenance margin requirement. Users can monitor this requirement via the\nportfolio margin ratio,\ndefined as\nThe account becomes liquidatable when portfolio_margin_ratio > 0.95. All notional values in the above definition are converted to USDC using\nborrow_oracle_price(token)\n.\nAfter borrow caps are hit, additional margin used must be supplied by the user using the settlement asset regardless of whether portfolio margin is active.\nDepending on the order of oracle price updates, either perp positions or spot borrows may be liquidated first. In other words, once portfolio margin ratio is liquidatable, users should not expect a deterministic liquidation sequence.\nPortfolio margin liquidations are taken over directly by the backstop liquidator at system address\n0xbbb...b\n. If the user health is below the full liquidation threshold, the supplied assets with positive LTV and borrowed assets are fully taken over by the backstop liquidator. If the user health is between the partial liquidation and full liquidation threshold, supplied assets with positive LTV and borrowed assets are instead taken over in 20% intervals, stopping as soon as the user is no longer liquidatable. The slippage threshold for takeover is\nsize_liquidated * oracle_price * (LTV(asset) + 2) / 3) * width\nwhere width is\n3M USDC\nfor HYPE and\n300k USDC\nfor BTC. The backstop liquidator converts collateral assets to debt assets for repayment, using a TWAP with a half life of 10 minutes. Unlike perps, there is no market liquidation phase, as spot books have less consistent liquidity than perps.\nPrevious\nAccount abstraction modes\nNext\nMargin tiers\nLast updated\n1 month ago"
    },
    "https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/robust-price-indices": {
      "title": "Robust price indices",
      "hash": "86e6f889116bb702f1ca2784e0435a3300c13188713268c1f37d917a183bc448",
      "last_checked": "2026-11-19T08:54:35.296313",
      "content": "Hyperliquid makes use of several robust prices based on order book and external data to minimize risk of market manipulation.\nOracle price\nis used to compute funding rates. This weighted median of CEX prices is robust because it does not depend on hyperliquid's market data at all. Oracle prices are updated by the validators approximately once every three seconds.\nMark price\nis the median of the following prices:\nOracle price plus a 150 second exponential moving average (EMA) of the difference between Hyperliquid's mid price and the oracle price\nThe median of best bid, best ask, last trade on Hyperliquid\nMedian of Binance, OKX, Bybit, Gate IO, MEXC perp mid prices with weights 3, 2, 2, 1, 1, respectively\nIf exactly two out of the three inputs above exist, the 30 second EMA of the median of best bid, best ask, and last trade on Hyperliquid is also added to the median inputs.\nMark price is an unbiased and robust estimate of the fair perp price, and is used for margining, liquidations, triggering TP/SL,\u00a0and computing unrealized pnl. Mark price is updated whenever validators publish new oracle prices. Therefore, mark and oracle price are updated approximately once every 3 seconds.\nThe EMA update formula is defined as follows for an update value of\nsample\nat duration\nt\nsince the last update\nema = numerator / denominator\nnumerator -> numerator * exp(-t / 2.5 minutes) + sample * t\ndenominator -> denominator * exp(-t / 2.5 minutes) + t\nPrevious\nMargin tiers\nNext\nLiquidations\nLast updated\n1 year ago"

    "https://hyperliquid.gitbook.io/hyperliquid-docs/trading/take-profit-and-stop-loss-orders-tp-sl": {
      "title": "Take profit and stop loss orders (TP/SL)",
      "hash": "cfe7b1056ef8408cc48c870e2d2ad8960d0139878086043e851b265d875f17d6",
      "last_checked": "2026-11-19T08:54:38.441175",
      "content": "TP/SL orders close your position when a certain profit (resp. loss) has realized on your position.\nThe\nmark price\nis used to trigger TP/SL orders.\nTP/SL orders can be dragged on the TradingView chart. Note that dragging in a way that causes the order to immediately execute will lead to an error. Usually this prevents user mistakes, but if this is your desired behavior you can manually close the order from the position table or order form.\nLimit vs Market TP/SL orders\nUsers can choose between TP/SL market and limit orders. TP/SL market orders have a slippage tolerance of 10%.\nBy setting the limit price on TP/SL orders, users can control the slippage tolerance of a triggered order. The more aggressive the limit price, the more likely the TP/SL order will be filled upon triggering, but the higher the potential slippage upon filling.\nAs a concrete example, a SL order to close a long with trigger price $10 and limit price $10 will hit the book when the mark price drops below $10. If the price drops from $11 to $9 instantly it is quite likely this SL order would rest at\u00a0$10 instead of filling. However, if the limit price were $8 instead of $10, it's likely to fill at some price between $9 and $8.\nTP/SL associated with a position\nTP/SL opened from the position form will have a size equal to the entire position by default. These orders will attempt to close the entire position at the time of trigger. If a specific size is configured on these TP/SL orders, they will be fixed-sized (i.e. they will not resize with the position after being placed).\nTP/SL associated with a parent order (a.k.a one-cancels-other, OCO)\nThis style of TP/SL is more complicated. Read the below carefully to avoid unexpected outcomes.\nTP/SL opened from the order form have a fixed size equal to the order they are tied to.\nIf the parent order is fully filled at placement, the children TP and/or SL orders are immediately placed. This behavior is similar to the TP/SL assocated with a position.\nWhen the parent order is not fully filled, the children orders enter an untriggered state. The TP/SL orders have not been placed, and upon cancelation of an unfilled parent order, the child TP/SL orders will be canceled.\nIf the trader cancels a partially filled parent order,\nthe child TP/SL orders are fully canceled as well\n. If the trader desires a TP/SL for the partially filled size, they must do so manually, e.g. by placing a separate TP/SL orders associated with the new position.\nHowever, if the parent order is partially filled and then canceled due to insufficient margin, the TP/SL orders will be placed as if the order were fully filled.\nIn conclusion,\nchildren TP/SL orders associated with a parent order will be placed if and only if the parent order fully fills or is partially filled followed by a cancelation for insufficient margin\n.\nPrevious\nOrder types\nNext\nEntry price and pnl\nLast updated\n3 months ago"
    }

    "https://docs.lighter.xyz/api": {
      "title": "API Reference",
      "hash": "9bb67319b09e8b8c5c80ac04d4160f7c4f812c6951927e5aef1ad478566accec",

      "content": "Copy\nOn this page\nBuy volume quota with USDC. Submit a signed L2Transfer of quota \u00d7 500 USDC base units to the volume quota payment account; the server validates it, sends it and credits the quota to the payer's L1 address (or destination_l1_address) once the transfer executes. If execution takes longer than about 5 seconds the response is status=pending and the purchase is claimed by calling again with tx_hash. Exactly one of tx_info (new purchase) or tx_hash (claim) is sent per call. Each transfer is credited once. Only Plus and Premium/Advanced accounts can receive quota, and the balance after the purchase may not exceed 30,000,000. See\nVolume Quota\n.\npost\nhttps://mainnet.zklighter.elliot.ai\nLighter mainnet\n/api/v1/buyVolumeQuota\nHeader parameters\nauthorization\nstring\nOptional\nAuth token in the Authorization header (or pass auth in the body).\nBody\napplication/x-www-form-urlencoded\nquota\ninteger \u00b7 int64\nRequired\nVolume quota to buy, in transactions. Price is quota \u00d7 500 USDC base units (1,000,000 quota = 500 USDC); at most 15,000,000 per request.\ntx_info\nstring\nOptional\nRequired for a new purchase (omit when claiming with tx_hash). Signed L2Transfer (tx type 12) of USDC (asset index 3), exactly quota \u00d7 500 USDC base units from the paying account to the volume quota payment account (index 765210 on Lighter, 51687 on Lighter on Robinhood Chain), as sent to sendTx. Omit when claiming a pending purchase with tx_hash.\ntx_hash\nstring\nOptional\nOptional; claim only, not needed for a normal purchase. Pass instead of tx_info to finish a purchase whose earlier call answered status=pending: the tx_hash from that response. No new payment is made. Credits that purchase once its transfer has executed; the payer must authenticate.\ndestination_l1_address\nstring\nOptional\nCredit this L1 address instead of the payer's own. Within each order, returned trade-type/role entries are sorted by their own last trade IDs. The flattened response is not necessarily globally sorted by each entry's\nlast_trade_id\n.\nIf more orders exist, the response includes\nnext_cursor\n. Pass it as\ncursor\nwith the same filters to get the next page. When\nnext_cursor\nis missing, you've reached the end.\nlimit\ncounts\norders\n. Apply it according to that channel's rules.\nerror: {code, message}\nRequest or subscription failed. Errors need not contain a\ntype\nfield; an optional\nid\nidentifies the request.\nSubscribe requests use slash-separated channel names; response\nchannel\nvalues use colons. Private channels take an\nauth\ntoken inside the subscribe message, as shown below. This WebSocket field is separate from REST authentication, which uses the\nAuthorization\nheader. Public-pool data does not require authentication; see\npool_data\nand\npool_info\n.\nStructure blocks below use placeholders such as\nINTEGER\n,\nSTRING\nand\nFLOAT\n; they describe payloads and are not literal JSON. Example response blocks are JSON.\ntransaction_time\nis in microseconds and may be omitted when no transaction execution time is available. See\nTimestamps\nfor field-specific units.\nKeepalive Requirements\nClients are responsible for keeping the connection alive by sending at least one frame every\n2 minutes\n. This can be either:\nA\nWebSocket ping frame\nAny application-level message\nClients sending\n{\"type\":\"ping\"}\nwill receive back\n{\"type\":\"pong\"}\n.\nIf the server receives no frames from a client within the 2-minute window, it will close the connection.\nSupport for\npermessage-deflate\ncompression is enabled.\nWrites to slow clients time out after 250 ms by default. Keep reading messages continuously; if the connection closes, reconnect and resubscribe to obtain fresh snapshots.\nSend Tx\nYou can send transactions using the websocket as follows:\nGitBook Assistant\nAsk\nCopy\n{\n\"type\": \"jsonapi/sendtx\",\n\"data\": {\n\"id\": STRING, // returned in the ws response\n\"tx_type\": INTEGER,\n\"tx_info\": ...\n}\n}\nThe\ntx_type\noptions can be found in the\nSignerClient\nfile, while\ntx_info\ncan be generated using the sign methods in the SignerClient.\nExample:\ncreate_modify_cancel_order_ws.py\nSend Batch Tx\nYou can send batch transactions to execute up to 50 transactions in a single message. Larger batches are rejected with error\n21514\n.\nGitBook Assistant\nAsk\nCopy\n{\n\"type\": \"jsonapi/sendtxbatch\",\n\"data\": {\n\"id\": STRING, // returned in the ws response\n\"tx_types\": \"[INTEGER]\",\n\"tx_infos\": \"[tx_info]\"\n}\n}\nThe\ntx_type\noptions can be found in the\nSignerClient\nfile, while\ntx_info\ncan be generated using the sign methods in the SignerClient.\nExample:\nws_send_batch_tx.py\nTypes\nThese types are shared across channels. orders for the given market in batches, every 50ms. While the nonce is tied to Lighter's matching engine, the offset is tied to the API servers; hence, you can expect the offset to change drastically on reconnection if you're routed to a different server. Regardless, on each update the offset will increase, but it's not guaranteed to be continuous. Additionally, this channel sends a complete snapshot on subscription, but only state changes after that. To verify the continuity of the data, you can check that\nbegin_nonce\non the current update matches the\nnonce\n(i.e.\nlast_nonce\n) of the previous update.\nGitBook
